Asynchronous dynamics of
\mathsf{SINTEGRITY}
.

Message Typing

\frac{}{\Psi_{0};z_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle,y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\vdash\mathbf{send}\,z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\beta}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}::y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle}\quad\textsc{right=$\otimes R$}\par\frac{}{\Psi_{0};z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\beta}{:}A{\langle c,e\rangle},x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B{\langle c,e\rangle}\vdash\mathbf{send}\,z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\beta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}::x_{\alpha+1}{:}B{\langle c,e\rangle}}\quad\textsc{right=$\multimap L$}\par\frac{k\in L}{\Psi_{0};y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}{\langle c,e\rangle}\vdash y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k::y_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}{\langle c,e\rangle}}\quad\textsc{right=$\oplus R$}\par\frac{k\in L}{\Psi_{0};x_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}{\langle c,e\rangle}\vdash(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P)::x_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}{\langle c,e\rangle}}\quad\textsc{right=$\&L$}\par\frac{}{\Psi_{0};\cdot\vdash\mathbf{close}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}::y_{\alpha}{:}1{\langle c,e\rangle}}\quad\textsc{right=$1R$}

10.4
Configuration typing

Similar to
[
23
]
, we allow configurations of processes to be
open
.
An open configuration can be
plugged
with other process configurations to form a closed program.
Open configurations are typed with the judgment
\Psi_{0};\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta^{\prime}
, indicating that the configuration
\mathcal{C}
provides sessions along the channels in
\Delta^{\prime}
, using sessions provided along channels in
\Delta
and given the concrete security lattice
\Psi_{0}
.

\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
are both linear contexts, consisting of actual run-time channels of the form
y_{i}{:}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle d_{i}^{\prime},e^{\prime}_{i}\rangle}
.
For brevity, we do not indicate a channel’s generation.
The typing rules are shown below.

\frac{}{\Psi_{0};x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle]\Vdash\cdot::(x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle])}\quad\textsc{right=$\mathbf{emp}$}\frac{\Psi_{0}\Vdash d_{1}\sqsubseteq d\quad\Psi_{0}\Vdash e_{1}\sqsubseteq e\quad\forall y_{\beta}{:}B[\langle d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\,(\Psi_{0}\Vdash d^{\prime}\sqsubseteq d)\quad\forall y_{\beta}{:}B[\langle d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\,(\Psi_{0}\Vdash e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq e)\quad\Psi_{0}\Vdash e_{1}\sqsubseteq d_{1}\quad\Psi_{0}\Vdash e\sqsubseteq d\quad\forall y_{\beta}{:}B[\langle d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\,(\Psi_{0}\Vdash e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq d^{\prime})\quad\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta\quad\Psi_{0};\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\vdash P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::(x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle])}{\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0},\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C},\mathbf{proc}(x_{\alpha}[\langle d,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)::(x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle])}\quad\textsc{right=$\mathbf{proc}$}\frac{\forall y_{\beta}{:}B[\langle d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\,(\Psi_{0}\Vdash d^{\prime}\sqsubseteq d)\quad\Psi_{0}\Vdash e\sqsubseteq d\quad\forall y_{\beta}{:}B[\langle d^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\,(\Psi_{0}\Vdash e^{\prime}\sqsubseteq d^{\prime})\quad\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta\quad\Psi_{0};\Delta^{\prime}_{0},\Delta\vdash M@\langle d,e\rangle::(x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle])}{\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0},\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C},\mathbf{msg}(M)::(x_{a}lpha{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle])}\quad\textsc{right=$\mathbf{msg}$}\frac{\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta\quad\Psi_{0};\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{1}::x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle]}{\Psi_{0};\Delta_{0},\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\Vdash\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{1}::\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}A[\langle d,e\rangle]}\quad\textsc{right=$\mathbf{comp}$}

Since
\Psi_{0}
and
\Sigma
are fixed, we may drop
\Psi_{0}
and
\Sigma
in a configuration typing judgment and a process typing judgment, respectively, for brevity.

10.5
Progress and preservation

Theorem 8

(Progress)
.

If
\Psi_{0};\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta^{\prime}
, then
either
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, or
\mathcal{C}
is poised.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the configuration typing of
\mathcal{C}
.
∎

Theorem 9

(Preservation)
.

If
\Psi_{0};\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, then
\Psi_{0};\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}^{\prime}::\Delta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is by considering different cases of
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and then by inversion on the typing derivations.
∎

11
Proof of the main lemma

11.1
Relevant nodes

We have three different kinds of nodes (i.e., processes and messages) in an open configuration
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}
:
relevant
nodes,
semi-relevant
nodes, and
irrelevant
nodes.
Relevant and semi-relevant nodes are processes or messages in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}
that can directly or indirectly influence what messages are made ready for transmission across the interface
{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}\Delta}\Vdash{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}K}
.
Relevant and semi-relevant nodes thus affect the
observable outcome
.
Irrelevant nodes, on the other hand, do not have any observable outcome.
What distinguishes a relevant node from a semi-relevant node is that a relevant node can directly cause an observable outcome,
whereas a semi-relevant node can only indirectly cause an observable outcome in combination with regrading.

Definition 10

(Running integrity of nodes)
.

The running integrity of a node is dependent on the kind of node:

1.

process: running integrity of process;

2.

negative message: min integrity of the message’s carrier channel;

3.

positive message: join of the min integrity of the message’s carrier channel with the running integrity of its parent (if it has any).

Definition 11

(Relevant channels and (semi-)relevant nodes)
.

Consider configuration
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}::K
and observer level
\xi
.

1.

A channel
x^{\langle c,e\rangle}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta\cup\{K\})
is relevant if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
.

2.

An offering channel
x^{\langle c,e\rangle}
of a node in
\mathcal{D}
is relevant, if
(a)
e\sqsubseteq\xi
and if
(b) there exists a node with running integrity
e_{0}\sqsubseteq\xi
and there exists a relevant channel
y

such that either both
x
and
y
are the node’s children or only one is a child of the node and the other is the node’s offering channel.

3.

A relevant node in
\mathcal{D}
has (a) a running integrity
e_{0}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
(b) a running confidentiality
c_{0}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
(c) at least one relevant channel as a child or as its offering channel.

4.

A semi-relevant node in
\mathcal{D}
has (a) a running integrity
e_{0}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
(b) a running confidentiality
c_{0}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and
(c) at least one relevant channel as a child or as its offering channel.

\mathcal{D}{\Downarrow}\xi
are the relevant and semi-relevant processes and messages in
\mathcal{D}
.
An irrelevant node has either a running integrity
e_{0}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or does not have a relevant channel as a child or its offering channel.
An irrelevant channel is not relevant.

In the proof of the fundamental theorem
Thm.

24
we must assert that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
will generate related outgoing observable messages
when stepped internally.
To make this assertion, we must know that the relevant and semi-relevant nodes in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
are in sync,
denoted by
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
{\Downarrow}\xi\equiv_{\xi}
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
{\Downarrow}\xi
,
indicating that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
run related program terms.

Def.

13
defines this notion precisely, and we discuss it below.
Before doing that, however, we must introduce the notion of a dead-end node, on which
Def.

13
relies.

Because our development uses an asynchronous semantics and because semi-relevant nodes are allowed to carry out unsynchronized actions,
not all the relevant and semi-relevant nodes in two program runs
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
are always in sync.
The notion of a
dead-end
node allows us to identify those relevant and semi-relevant nodes in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}

that are permitted to be out of sync because they are guaranteed to no longer cause any observable outcomes.

A relevant node becomes a dead-end node when it is waiting to receive a message from an irrelevant node.
Since the sends may not happen simultaneously in two program runs, or not at all,
the receiving relevant nodes may not receive the message simultaneously either.
But once the message is received, the receiving relevant nodes become irrelevant nodes themselves.
This scenario also holds true for semi-relevant nodes.
Semi-relevant nodes, however, additionally may be out of sync in two runs because either of them is carrying out unsynchronized sends
before being ready to receive from the irrelevant sender.

It may be helpful to briefly reflect upon why a relevant or semi-relevant node becomes irrelevant after receiving a message from an irrelevant node.
According to
Def.

11
, an irrelevant node’s running integrity is either
e_{0}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or
does not have any relevant channel.
In the former case, it must hold by the presuppositions for its offering channel that its min integrity is
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
,
increasing the running integrity of the recipient to at least
e
as well.
In the latter case, the offering channel of the irrelevant node must be irrelevant too (by virtue of the offering node being irrelevant).
Since it is connected to a relevant or semi-relevant receiver, its min integrity must be
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
,
thus increasing the running integrity of the recipient to at least
e
as well.

Below we define the notion of a dead-end node, which relies on process terms with an input prefix:

\downarrow_{x^{\langle c,e\rangle}}.P\,\triangleq\,\mathbf{case}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}(\ell{\Rightarrow}P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}\,\mid\,y{\leftarrow}\mathbf{recv}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle};P_{y}\,\mid\,\mathbf{wait}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle};P

The notion of a dead-end node also relies on the notion of a
path
between nodes,
which means that one node is reachable from the other by traversing edges in the tree.
An edge exists between two nodes if one is an ancestor of the other one.
We call a path
relevant
, if every node and edge along the path is relevant or semi-relevant.
We say that a node is in the interface
{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}\Delta}\Vdash{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}K}
,
if there exists a channel
x
such that either
x\in\Delta
and
x
is a resource of the node or

x\in K
and
x
is the node’s offering channel.

Definition 12

(Dead-end nodes)
.

Consider configuration
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}::K
and observer level
\xi
.

1.

A relevant or semi-relevant process
\mathbf{proc}(x\langle c,e\rangle,Q\,@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
in
\mathcal{D}
is a dead-end node,
if, for some
w
,
d_{2}
, and
e_{2}
such that
e_{2}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and where
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
by assumption, either

•

\Psi\vDash\downarrow_{w^{\langle d_{2},e_{2}\rangle}}.P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q_{\mid\xi}
, for some
d_{s}
and
P
such that
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi

where
Q_{\mid\xi}
is equal to
Q
modulo renaming of channels with min integrity higher than or incomparable to the observer, or

•

Q=\downarrow_{w^{\langle d_{2},e_{2}\rangle}}.Q^{\prime}
, for some
Q^{\prime}
.

2.

A positive relevant or semi-relevant message
\mathbf{msg}(P)
in
\mathcal{D}
is a dead-end node,
if its parent is a dead-end node.

3.

A relevant or semi-relevant node in
\mathcal{D}
is a dead-end node, if any relevant path from the node to a node
in the interface
{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}\Delta}\Vdash{\color[rgb]{0.0898,0.4258,0.0859}K}

includes at least one dead-end node (i.e., the path that make the process relevant or semi-relevant includes a dead-end node.)

Now with the definition of a dead-end node in place, we can finally define when to program runs
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
are in sync.

Definition 13

(Synchronized program runs)
.

We write
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi\equiv_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
iff

1.

for any relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1},

there is a relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}

such that
\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)=\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
upto renaming of high-integrity channels. In particular, we have
Q=P_{\mid\xi}
and if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, we have
x=y
:

\Psi;\Delta\vdash P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::x[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,\mathsf{and}\,\Psi;\Delta_{\mid\xi}\vdash P_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::y[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,

where
∣ξ
stands for a renaming of channels with integrity
e^{\prime}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

2.

for any relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1},

there is a relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}.

3.

for any relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2},

that is not a dead-end process in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, there is a relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1}

such that
\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)=\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
upto renaming of high-integrity channels. In particular, we have
Q=P_{\mid\xi}
and if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, we have
x=y
:

\Psi;\Delta\vdash P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::x[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,\mathsf{and}\,\Psi;\Delta_{\mid\xi}\vdash P_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::y[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,

where
∣ξ
stands for a renaming of channels with integrity
e^{\prime}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

4.

for any relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2},

which is not a dead-end message in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, there is a relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1}.

5.

for any semi-relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1},

there is a semi-relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}

such that
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and the two nodes agree on the channels with less than or equal integrity to the observer, e.g., if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
x=y
, and

\Psi;\Delta\vdash P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::x[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,\mathsf{and}\,\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}\vdash Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::y[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,

where
\Delta^{\prime}\Downarrow d_{s}=\Delta\Downarrow d_{s}
; meaning that any channel in
\Delta
with integrity
e
with
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e
, also occurs in
\Delta^{\prime}
and vice-versa.

6.

for any semi-relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1},

which is sending along
\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle
, there is a semi-relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M^{\prime})\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2},

such that
\Psi\Vdash M\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}M^{\prime}
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and the two nodes agree on the channels connected to the messages.

7.

for any semi-relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(x[\langle c,e\rangle],P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2},

which is not a dead-end process in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, there is a semi-relevant node

\mathbf{proc}(y[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1}

such that
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and the two nodes agree on the channels with less than or equal integrity to the observer, e.g., if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
x=y
, and

\Psi;\Delta\vdash P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::x[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,\mathsf{and}\,\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}\vdash Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle::y[\langle c,e\rangle]:B,

where
\Delta^{\prime}\Downarrow d_{s}=\Delta\Downarrow d_{s}
; meaning that any channel in
\Delta
with integrity
e
with
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e
, also occurs in
\Delta^{\prime}
and vice-versa.

8.

for any semi-relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M)\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2},

which is sending along
x\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle
and is not a dead-end message in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, there is a semi-relevant node

\mathbf{msg}(M^{\prime})\,\,\textit{in}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{1},

such that
\Psi\Vdash M\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}M^{\prime}
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and the two nodes agree on the channels connected to the messages.

We say a channel is connected to a node if either the node offers the channel or uses it as a resource.

11.2
Main lemma

Having defined when two program runs are in sync,
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi\equiv_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
,
we state and prove a lemma that asserts that the program runs remain synced along internal transitions.
This lemma is used in the proof of the fundamental theorem
Thm.

24
and states,
for two synced program runs
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, that if
\mathcal{D}_{1}
takes an internal step to
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime}
,
there exists a way to step
\mathcal{D}_{2}
to
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\prime}
remain synced.
The existential indicates that we assume a non-deterministic scheduler.

Lemma 14

(Catch-up)
.

Consider
\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{i}::K
such that
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi\equiv_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.
If
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
, then for some
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, we have
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi\equiv_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

Proof.

The proof is by cases on the possible
\mapsto
steps. We consider different cases based on the semantic rule with which
\mathcal{D}_{1}
steps. In each case we prove that either the step does not change relevancy of any process in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
or we can step
\mathcal{D}_{2}
such that the same change of relevancy occurs in it too.

Case 1.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider subcases based on relevancy of process offering along
y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle]
:

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
is irrelevant.

By inversion on the typing rules
e_{1}\sqsubseteq e
. By definition either
e_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels connected to the process including its offering channel
y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}
are relevant. In both cases

\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle),

and

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)

are irrelevant in the post step.
The reason is: from
e_{1}\sqsubseteq e
and
e_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, we get
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. Channel
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
is not relevant in the pre-step, and both channels
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
and
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
are not relevant in pre-step and post-step configurations.

Every irrelevant resource of
\mathbf{proc}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha},y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@d_{1})
will remain irrelevant in the post-step too.

In this subcase, our goal is to show

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi\end{array}

To prove this we need two observations:

•

\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
are irrelevant and they will be dismissed by the projection. (As explained above.)

•

Replacing
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
with these two nodes, does not affect relevancy of the rest of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
:

The relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
remains intact since channel
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
is irrelevant in both pre-step and post-step.

The relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
remains intact as we replace their irrelevant root with another irrelevant process. In particular, this step does not change relevancy of any chain positive messages that process
P
might have as (grand) children.

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
is semi-relevant, i.e.,
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

By assumption (
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
):

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
\Psi\Vdash y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, which means that
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{1}
.

Subcase 2a
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e
.

we get that
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, meaning that the channel
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
is not relevant in the pre-step and channels
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
and
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
are not relevant in the post-step. Moreover,
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant in the post step.

Here, without loss of generality, we assume that when in the pattern checking two
Unsync
rules are available, we apply the one that works on the first process.

In this subcase, our goal is to show

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi\end{array}

By
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
we know
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{1}
, and by the assumption of this subcase we know
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e
. Thus
by inversion, we know that the last rule in the derivation is
Brb-unsync
1
, and we get
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q
. Processes that offer along
u_{\alpha}
and
z_{\delta}
in the post-step remain semi-relevant as their running integrity does not change, and their relevant channels in the resources remain intact.

The relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
remains intact since
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
is neither relevant in the pre-step, nor the post-step.

The relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
remains intact as we replace their semi-relevant root with another semi-relevant process.

Subcase 2b
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e

.
If
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, we have
y_{\alpha}=z_{\delta}
.

We apply induction on the size of
Q
to show that for some
Q^{\prime}
and some
\mathcal{D}_{3}
we have

\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{3}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.j;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)

where every node in
\mathcal{D}_{3}
is irrelevant, the process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
in the post-step is semi-relavant, we have
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q^{\prime}
.

We proceed by inversion on the derivation for
\Psi\Vdash y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q
. Since
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{1}
,
we know that the last rule in the derivation is one of the following three, i.e. either
Brb-SndLab
, or
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
2
, or
Brb-Unsync
2
.

•

The first case
(
Brb-SndLab
). We get exactly what we want, i.e. process
Q
is of the form,

y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.j;Q^{\prime},

and

\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{0}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.j;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle),

where we rename
z_{\delta}
for
y_{\alpha}
, if
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi

•

In
the second case (
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
2
)
, we know that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)=\\
\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],(u^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}\leftarrow X\leftarrow\Delta)@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle;Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

where
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{0}
and
\forall w{:}B_{w}[\langle c_{w},e_{w}\rangle]\in\Delta
we have
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{w}
. Moreover,
\Psi\Vdash z_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q^{1}
. By the dynamic rule for spawn, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],(u^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}\leftarrow X\leftarrow\Delta)@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle;Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto\\[5.0pt]
\mathbf{proc}(u_{0}[\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle],[x_{0},\Delta/x,\mathsf{dom}(\Delta^{\prime})]\hat{\gamma}(Q^{2})@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle)\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

where
(\Psi^{\prime};\Delta^{\prime}\vdash X=Q_{2}@\langle\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\rangle::u{[\langle\psi_{3},\psi_{4}\rangle]}:A^{\prime})
is defined in the signature
\Sigma
.

Observe that (i)
e_{0}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, since
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{0}\sqsubseteq e
(the condition
e_{0}\sqsubseteq e
is by well-typedness), and thus the spawned process is irrelevant. (ii) The process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
remains semi-relevant: its running integrity does not change and the channels passed to the caller are all not relevant since
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{w}
and
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. (iii) The resources of the process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
that have integrity
e_{r}
with
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{r}
remain intact. (iv) We have
\Psi\Vdash y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q^{1}
.

By the above observations, we can apply the induction hypothesis on
Q^{1}
with a smaller size to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],(u^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}\leftarrow X\leftarrow\Delta)@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle;Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto\\[5.0pt]
\mathbf{proc}(u_{0}[\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle],[x_{0},\Delta/x,\mathsf{dom}(\Delta^{\prime})]\hat{\gamma}(Q^{2})@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle)\,\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{*}\\[4.0pt]
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{3}\,\mathbf{proc}(u_{0}[\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle],[x_{0},\Delta/x,\mathsf{dom}(\Delta^{\prime})]\hat{\gamma}(Q^{2})@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle)\,\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.j;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

By
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{0}
, we have
e_{0}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
and thus the spawned process is irrelevant. By the induction hypothesis, every other process in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{3}
is irrelevant.

•

In
the third case (
Brb-Unsync
2
)
, we know that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)=\\
\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle},\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

where
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{u}
. Moreover,
\Psi\Vdash z_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q^{1}
. By assumption (
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e
) we know that
u_{\beta}
is a resource and not the offering channel. By the dynamic rules for sending messages, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto\\[5.0pt]
\mathbf{msg}(\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.\_)\,\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],[u_{\beta+1}/u_{\beta}]Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

Observe that (i)
u_{\beta}
is not a relevant channel (by
d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{u}
and
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
), and thus the message is irrelevant. (ii) The process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
remains semi-relevant: its running integrity does not change and (in the case of higher-order sends) the channel passed to the message is not relevant since they are of the same secrecy
e_{u}
. (iii) The resources of the process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
that have integrity
e_{r}
with
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{r}
remain intact. (iv) We have
\Psi\Vdash y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k;P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}[u_{\beta+1}/u_{\beta}]Q^{1}
.

By the above observations, we can apply the induction hypothesis on
[u_{\beta+1}/u_{\beta}]Q^{1}
with a smaller size to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto\\[5.0pt]
\mathbf{msg}(\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.\_)\,\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],[u_{\beta+1}/u_{\beta}]Q^{1}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{*}\\[4.0pt]
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{3}\,\mathbf{msg}(\uparrow_{u_{\beta}^{\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle}}.\_)\,\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.j;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\end{array}

By the induction hypothesis, every other process in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{3}
is irrelevant.

Now that we established that

\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{3}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.j;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)

where every node in
\mathcal{D}_{3}
is irrelevant, the process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
in the post-step is semi-relavant, and we have
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{1}\rangle}Q^{\prime}
, we have:

\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{3}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.j)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}

If
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
z_{\delta}=y_{\alpha}
, otherwise both messages in the first and second run are irrelevant. Note that by the typing rules, we know that
d_{1}\sqsubseteq c
, and by
d_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, we get
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and thus the produced messages cannot be relevant (they are either semi-relevant or irrelevant). It is straightforward to observe that the two post-step configurations remain related; explained below:

In this step, the process offering along
z_{\delta+1}
may become dead-end in the post-step of the second run and as a result transforms chain of positive messages attached to it to dead-end. This means that we need to satisfy less conditions for the process and its connected channels, i.e., the conditions (7) and (8) do not need to be satisfied anymore.

We consider two subcacses based on the level
e
:

•

If
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{msg}(z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.j)
are irrelevant in both runs, and will be dismissed by the projections. Moreover, in this case neither
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
, nor
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}
are relevant in the pre-step and post-step configurations. Thus the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
will remain intact.
Similarly, the relevancy of nodes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
remain intact as we replace their semi-relevant root with another semi-relevant process with the same set of resources. In particular, this step does not change relevancy of any chain positive messages that process
P
might have as (grand) children.

•

If
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
are relevant in the pre-step in both runs. In the post-step, channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta+1}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
are relevant in both runs.

Relevancy of messages
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
in the post-steps are determined by the running integrities (
e^{\prime}_{1}
and
e^{\prime}_{2}
) of their parents (
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
) in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
.

First observe that if
e^{\prime}_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, then the message is semi-relevant in the post-step of the first run and its parent is either relevant or semi-relevant. Thus the correponding parent (
N_{2}
) occurs in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
with
e^{\prime}_{2}\sqsubseteq\xi
, which is also either relevant or semi-relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. And as a result, the message is semi-relevant in the post-step of the second run.
Thus messages
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.j)
are semi-relevant in both runs and channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
are relevant in the post-step too, satisfying condition (6) in Definition
13
.

Otherwise, if
e^{\prime}_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
,
the parent (
N_{1}
) is irrelevant and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant in the post step of the first run, and will be dismissed by the projection. We satisfy condition (6) trivially.

Now consider the case where the message is not dead-end in the second configuration, meaning that its (grand-)parent process in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is not dead-end, then both messages are semi-relevant in the post-step and we satisfy the condition (8) in Definition
13
too.

Finally, consider the case where the message is dead-end in the second configurations, meaning that its (grand-)parent process in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is dead-end, then there is no need to satisfy condition (8) in Definition
13
.

In all the above cases the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
remain intact and thus they continue to be related.

Similarly, the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
remain intact and thus they continue to be related.

Subcase 3.
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
is relevant, i.e.,
e_{1}\sqsubseteq d_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
.

By assumption (
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
):

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.k;P_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
P_{\mid\xi}
is equal to
P
modulo renaming of some channels with higher or incomparable integrity to the observer and if
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
z_{\delta}=y_{\alpha}
. We have

\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta+1}[c],[z^{c}_{\delta+1}/z^{c}_{\delta}]P_{\mid\xi}@d_{1})\mathbf{msg}(z^{c}_{\delta}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}

It is enough to show

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta+1}[\langle c,e\rangle],[z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta+1}/z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}]P_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathbf{msg}(z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

In this step, the process offering along
z_{\delta+1}
may become dead-end in the post-step of the second run and as a result transforms chain of positive messages attached to it to dead-end. This means that we need to satisfy less conditions for the process and its connected channels, i.e., the conditions (3) and (4) do not need to be satisfied anymore.

We consider two subcacses based on the level
e
:

•

If
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{msg}(z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}.k)
are irrelevant in both runs, and will be dismissed by the projections. Moreover, in this case neither
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
, nor
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}
are relevant in the pre-step and post-step configurations. Thus the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
will remain intact.

It remains to show that projections of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are equal in the post-step too.
The relevancy of nodes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
remain intact as we replace their relevant root with another relevant process with the same set of resources. In particular, this step does not change relevancy of any chain positive messages that process
P
might have as (grand) children.

•

If
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, then channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
are relevant in the pre-step in both runs. In the post-step, channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta+1}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
are relevant in both runs.

Relevancy of messages
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
in the post-steps are determined by the running integrities (
e^{\prime}_{1}
and
e^{\prime}_{2}
) of their parents (
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
) in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
.

First observe that if
e^{\prime}_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, then message is relevant in the post-step of the first run and its parent is either relevant or semi-relevant. Thus the correponding parent (
N_{2}
) occurs in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
with
e^{\prime}_{2}\sqsubseteq\xi
, which is also either relevant or semi-relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. And as a result, the message is relevant in post-step of the second run: we satisfy condition (2) in Definition
13
.

Otherwise, if
e\not\sqsubseteq e^{\prime}_{1}
and
e^{\prime}_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, then the parent (
N_{1}
) is either relevant or semi-relevant in the first run and by assumption there is a corresponding relevant or semi-relevant parent (
N_{2}
) in the second run. Thus messages
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
are relevant in both runs and channels
z^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}=y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
are relevant in the post-step too, satisfying Condition (2) in Definition
13
.

If
e\not\sqsubseteq e^{\prime}_{1}
and
e^{\prime}_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
,
then the parent (
N_{1}
) is irrelevant and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant in the post step of the first run, and will be dismissed by the projection.

In all the above cases the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
remain intact and thus they continue to be related.

Similarly, the relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
remain intact and thus they continue to be related.

Case 2.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(w_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@(\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle))\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

We consider sub-cases based on relevancy of process offering along
x^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{\beta}
.

Subcase 1.
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
is irrelevant.

By definition either
g_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
or none of the channels connected to
P
including the channel
y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}
are relevant. In both cases the message
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
in the pre-step and the process

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)

in the post-step are irrelevant too.
Moreover, channel
x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle]
is irrelevant in the pre-step (by the tree invariant we know that
g_{1}\sqsubseteq g
), and will remain irrelevant in the post-step.

In this subcase, our goal is to show

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

To prove this we need four observations:

•

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)
is irrelevant and will be dismissed by the projection (as explained above).

•

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant in the pre-step (in particular, it is not a corresponding node for a relevant or semi-relevant node in the second run as specified in conditions 4 and 8 in Definition
13
). The reason is: if the message is either semi-relevant or relevant then its running integrity
g_{1}\sqcup e\sqsubseteq\xi
and its connected channels are relevant. This contradicts with the process offering along
x_{\beta}
being irrelevant in the pre-step.

•

Replacing
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
with

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle),

does not affect the relevancy of the rest of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
since channel
x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\beta}
is irrelevant in both pre-step and post-step.

•

Replacing
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
with

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle),

does not affect the relevancy of the rest of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
either:

we replace their irrelevant root, either

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)\,\,\,\mathsf{or}\,\,\,\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)

with another irrelevant node,
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)
. In particular, this step does not change relevancy of any chain of positive messages that process
P
might have as (grand) children:

–

If
g_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, then any chain of messages connected to the process is irrelevant in the pre-step, nor the post-step.

–

If
g_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
but the process does not have any relevant channels connected to it in the pre-step, we consider the min integrity
e_{m}
of the chain of messages connected to the process. If
e_{m}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, the chain of messages is irrelevant in the pre-step, and in the post-step.
Otherwise, if
e_{m}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we know that the channels connecting the messages in the chain and the chain to the process oferring along
x_{\beta}
are irrelevant in the pre-step (otherwise we could form a contradiction with the process offering along
x_{\beta}
being irrelevant in the pre-step). The channels remain irrelevant in the post-step too, as we only (may) increase the running integrity of the messages in the chain.

In particular, none of the messages in these chains of messages are the corresponding node for a relevant or semi-relevant node in the second run (for satisfying conditions
4
and
8
in Definition
13
).

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
is semi-relevant, i.e.
g_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
d_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

By assumption (
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
):

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
\Psi\Vdash\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, which means that
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq g_{1}
.
We proceed by considering three subcases based on the relevancy of
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
: it is either irrelevant, semi-relevant, or relevant.

Subcase 2a.

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant, i.e.,
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

In this case, the process offering along
x_{\beta}
in the post-step of the first configuration is irrelevant. Moreover, by assumption, we know that the process offering along
z_{\delta}
in the second configuration is dead-end. Our goal is to show:

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

To prove this we need the following observations:

•

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)
is irrelevant and will be dismissed by the projection.

•

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is irrelevant in the pre-step (in particular, it is not a corresponding node for a relevant or semi-relevant node in the second run as specified in conditions 4 and 8 in Definition
13
).

•

By the tree invariant,
e\sqcup g_{1}\sqsubseteq g
and channel
x^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{\beta}
is irrelevant in both pre-step and post-step.
As a result, replacing
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
and
\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
in the pre-step with

\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)
in the post-step, does not affect the relevancy of the rest of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
.

•

However, replacing
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
and

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
with
\mathbf{proc}(x_{\beta}[\langle d,g\rangle],[y_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c,e\rangle}/y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}]P_{k}@\langle d_{1}\sqcup c,g_{1}\sqcup e\rangle)
, may affect the relevancy of the rest of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
. We explain below how we preserve relatedness of two cnofigurations despite this change:

For those subtrees in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
that are connected to the process that offers along
x_{\beta}
in the pre-step via an irrelevant channel, the relevancy does not change in the pre-and post-step. This, in particular, holds for the subtree offering along
v
.

If a subtree that is connected to the process via a relevant channel, then by the tree invariant, we know that all nodes in the sub-tree are either relevant or semi-relevant. The interesting case is when this channel becomes irrelevant and consequently makes all the nodes in the sub-tree irrelevant too. Next, we consider this case for an arbitrary channel
u[c_{u},e_{u}]
and sub-tree
\mathcal{D}_{u}
that offers this channel, where
e_{u}\sqsubseteq\xi
. If the nodes in
\mathcal{D}_{u}
become irrelevant, then they are dismissed by the projection in the post-step, so there is no need to establish conditions 1,2,5, and 6 in Definition
13
for them. We show that these nodes are not corresponding nodes to any process/message in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
in the pre-step as required by conditions 3,4,7, and 8 in Definition
13
: Assume they are, we get that there is a sub-tree
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{u}
offering along
u[c_{u},e_{u}]
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. By relatedness of the configurations and since
u
is a relevant channel, we know that
u
is a resource of the process offering along
z_{\delta}
in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{D}_{u}
becomes irrelevant in the post-step, we know that its only connection to the interface in the pre-step is via the process offering along
x_{\beta}
in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. Thus,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{u}
has the same property: its only connection to the interface is via the process offering along
z_{\delta}
, meaning that all nodes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{u}
are dead-ends and do no have a counterpart in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{u}
in the pre-step, which is a contradiction.
This completes the proof of this subcase.

Subcase 2b.

\mathbf{msg}(y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
is semi-relevant, i.e.,
e\sqsubseteq\xi
, and
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.
By assumption, we know that

We use assumption

to show that for some

and some

we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathcal{D}_{3}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],\mathbf{\mathbf{}}{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow Q^{\prime}_{\ell\mid\xi})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\end{array}

where every node in

.
Similar to Case 1. Subcase 2b, we apply induction on the size of

and inversion on the derivation for
\Psi\Vdash\mathbf{case}\,y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
to show the above.

Since

we know that the last rule in the derivation is one of the following three, i.e. either

, or

2

•

In
the first case
(

), we get exactly what we want, i.e. process

is of the form,

and

\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{0}

Moreover, for every
j,\ell\in L
, we have
\Psi\Vdash P_{\ell}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\sqcup g\rangle}Q^{\prime}_{j}
.

•

The second case (
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
2
)
. see Case 1. Subcase 2b.

•

The third case (
Brb-Unsync
2
)
.
see Case 1. Subcase 2b.

Thus we can step the second configuration as

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\\

We know that
\Psi\Vdash P_{k}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\sqcup g\rangle}Q^{\prime}_{j}
. By assumption, the process offering along
x_{\beta}
in the post-step of the first run is semi-relevant and so is the process offering along
z_{\delta}
in the post-step of the second run. The process offering along
z_{\delta}
in the post-step of the second run may become dead-end and makes some other ndoes dead-end, but this only means we need to establish less conditions. The relevancy of other processes and messages in the post-step of the first and second run remains intact, and they continue to be reltaed by Definition

.

Case 3.
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

To prove this we need two observations:

•

Both processes in the post-step

•

is irrelevant in both pre-step and post-step.

The relevancy of processes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
remains intact as we replace their irrelevant root with another irrelevant process. In particular, this step does not change relevancy of any chain positive messages that process
P
might have as (grand) children.

Subcase 2.
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
is semi-relevant.

By assumption (
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
):

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

such that
\Psi\Vdash(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
for some
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, which means that
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq g_{1}
.

Subcase 2a
d_{s}\sqsubseteq d_{2}
and
\forall u:B_{u}\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle\in\Lambda.d_{s}\sqsubseteq e_{u}
.

we get that
d_{2}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and
\forall u:B_{u}\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle\in\Lambda.e_{u}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, meaning that the channels in
\Lambda
and
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}
are irrelevant in the pre-step and post-step.
Moreover, the newly spawned channel
a_{0}^{\langle d,g\rangle}
is irrelevant in the post-step, and the process spawned in the post-step
\mathbf{proc}(a_{0}[\langle d,g\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0},\Lambda/x^{\langle d,g\rangle},\Lambda^{\prime}]\hat{\gamma}\,P@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle)
is irrelevant.

In this subcase, our goal is to show

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(a_{0}[\langle d,g\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0},\Lambda/x^{\langle d,g\rangle},\Lambda^{\prime}]\hat{\gamma}\,P@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle),\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0}/x^{\langle d,g\rangle}]P^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi\end{array}

By inversion, we know that the last rule in the derivation is
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
1
, and we get
\Psi\Vdash P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
.
In paricular, they have the same set of relevant resources (but not necessarily the same set of irrelevant resources).
The relevancy of modes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
does not change since by the tree-invariant the channel
y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}
is irrelevant in the pre-step and remains irrelevant in the post-step.

The relevancy of nodes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
does not change either since in this spawn we only pass irrelevant nodes as resources to the spawnee.

Subcase 2b
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq d_{2}
or
\exists u:B_{u}\langle c_{u},e_{u}\rangle\in\Lambda.\,d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq e_{u}
.

We use the assumption
\Psi\Vdash(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
to show that for some
Q^{\prime}
and some
\mathcal{D}_{3}
we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathcal{D}_{3}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],(w^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\end{array}

where every node in
\mathcal{D}_{3}
is irrelevant, the process offering along
z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle]
in the post-step is semi-relavant, and
\Psi\Vdash[a_{0}/x]P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\sqcup g\rangle}[a_{0}/w]Q^{\prime}
.
Similar to Case 1. Subcase 2b, we apply induction on the size of
Q
and inversion on the derivation for
\Psi\Vdash(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}Q
to show the above.

Since
d_{s}\not\sqsubseteq g_{1}
,
we know that the last rule in the derivation is one of the following three, i.e. either
Brb-Spawn
, or
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
2
, or
Brb-Unsync
2
.
The proof in the last two cases are identical to the corresponding cases in Case 1. Subcase 2b. We only provide the proof for the first case:

•

In
the first case
(
Brb-Spawn
), we get exactly what we want, i.e. process
Q
is of the form,

(w^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi},

and

\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],Q_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mapsto^{0}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],(w^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle).

Moreover, we have
\Psi\Vdash[a_{0}/x]P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\sqcup g\rangle}[a_{0}/w]Q^{\prime}
.

•

The second case (
Brb-Unsync-Spawn
2
)
. see Case 1. Subcase 2b.

•

The third case (
Brb-Unsync
2
)
.
see Case 1. Subcase 2b.

Thus we can step the second configuration as

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\\
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{3}\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle d,g\rangle],(w^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;Q^{\prime}_{\mid\xi}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto\\
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{3}\,\mathbf{proc}(a_{0}[\langle d,g\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0},\Lambda/w^{\langle d,g\rangle},\Lambda^{\prime}]\hat{\gamma}\,P@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle),\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0}/w^{\langle d,g\rangle}]Q^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\end{array}

(we assume that the fresh channel being spawned will be
a
in the second run too. Moreover, the (unique) substitution

\hat{\gamma}
is the same when stepping both runs.)

We know that
\Psi\Vdash[a_{0}/x]P^{\prime}\sim_{\langle d_{s},g_{1}\rangle}[a_{0}/w]Q^{\prime}
.

There are three sub-cases for the spawned processes in the two runs: they can be both relevant, or they are both semi-relevant, or they are both irrelevant. In the first case the spawned processes are related by the conditions of Definition
13
trivially. In the second case, we use Lemma
15
to show that the spawned processes are related by the conditions of Definition
13
. In the third case, the spawned processes are both discarded by the projections.

We may introduce dead-end nodes in the post-step of the second configuration, but this only means we need to establish less conditions.

It is straightforward to see that the rest of the nodes continue to be reltaed by Definition
13
.

Subcase 3.
\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],(x^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)
is relevant.

By assumption we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta}[\langle c,e\rangle],(w^{\langle d,g\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Lambda)@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle;P^{\prime}_{\mid\xi})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\end{array}

Thus we can step the second configuration as

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{3}\,\mathbf{proc}(a_{0}[\langle d,g\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0},\Lambda/w^{\langle d,g\rangle},\Lambda^{\prime}]\hat{\gamma}\,P@\langle d_{2},g_{2}\rangle),\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha}[\langle c,e\rangle],[a^{\langle d,g\rangle}_{0}/w^{\langle d,g\rangle}]P^{\prime}@\langle d_{1},g_{1}\rangle)\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\end{array}

(we assume that the fresh channel being spawned will be
a
in the second run too. Moreover, the (unique) substitution

\hat{\gamma}
is the same when stepping both runs.)

There are three sub-cases for the spawned processes in the two runs: they can be both relevant, or they are both semi-relevant, or they are both irrelevant. In the first case the spawned processes are related by the conditions of Definition
13
trivially. In the second case, we use Lemma
15
to show that the spawned processes are related by the conditions of Definition
13
. In the third case, the spawned processes are both discarded by the projections.

We may introduce dead-end nodes in the post-step of the second configuration, but this only means we need to establish less conditions.

It is straightforward to see that the rest of the nodes continue to be reltaed by Definition
13
.

∎

11.3
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma 15
.

For every
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\Psi};\Delta\vdash_{\Sigma}(P){@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle}::x{:}A\langle c,e\rangle
and confidentiality level
d_{s}\sqsubseteq c_{0}
, we have
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{0}\rangle}P
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the typing derivation for
P
. We consider the last step in the derivation. The only interesting case is when
P
is a case, i.e.,
P=\mathbf{case}\,w^{\langle d_{2},e_{2}\rangle}(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}
. To establish the induction hypothesis, we need to know that for all
i,j\in L
we have
\Psi\Vdash P_{i}\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{0}\sqcup e_{2}\rangle}P_{j}
. By inversion on the typing derivation, we know that
\Psi\Vdash P_{i}\sim_{\langle d_{2}\sqcup c_{0},e_{0}\sqcup e_{2}\rangle}P_{j}
, where
d_{s}\sqsubseteq d_{2}\sqcup c_{0}
. By Lemma
16
, we have
\Psi\Vdash P_{i}\sim_{\langle d_{s},e_{0}\sqcup e_{2}\rangle}P_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

If
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{1},e_{0}\rangle}Q
and
\Psi\Vdash d_{2}\sqsubseteq d_{1}
, then
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{2},e_{0}\rangle}Q
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the derivation for
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{1},e_{0}\rangle}Q
.
We look at the condition for
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{2},e_{0}\rangle}Q
, if the last step is a synchronized step, we can apply the induction hypothesis and apply the same rule on the result. If not, an assumption of the form
\Psi\Vdash d_{1}\sqsubseteq e^{\prime}
holds in the given derivation. By
\Psi\Vdash d_{2}\sqsubseteq d_{1}
, we get
\Psi\Vdash d_{2}\sqsubseteq e^{\prime}
; thus the assumption holds for
d_{2}
too and we can apply the induction hypothesis and apply the same rule to build a derivation for
\Psi\Vdash P\sim_{\langle d_{2},e_{0}\rangle}Q
.
∎

12
Logical relation

Definition 17
.

The configuration
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta^{\prime}
is ready to send along
\Lambda\subseteq\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
iff for all
y_{\alpha}{:}C\in\Lambda
, there is a message
\mathbf{msg}(M)
in
\mathcal{C}
sending along
y_{\alpha}
, i.e.
M
is of the form
y_{\alpha}.k
,
\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}\,y_{\alpha}
, or
\mathbf{close}\,y_{\alpha}
.

Similarly, the configuration
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}::\Delta^{\prime}
is ready to receive along
\Lambda\subseteq\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}
iff for all
y_{\alpha}{:}C\in\Lambda
, there is a process
\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},P)
in
\mathcal{C}
waiting to receive along
y_{\alpha}
, i.e.
P
is of the form
\mathbf{case}\,y_{\alpha}(\ell\Rightarrow Q_{\ell})_{\ell\in I}
,
w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}y_{\alpha};Q
, or
\mathbf{wait}\,y_{\alpha};Q
.

Definition 18
.

The set
\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, is defined as all channels with the sending semantics in
\Delta,K
, i.e.,
y_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)
iff for some positive type
T
, we have
y_{\alpha}{:}T\in\Delta
or for some negative type
T
, we have
y_{\alpha}{:}T\in K
.

Similarly, the set
\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, is defined as all channels with the receiving semantics in
\Delta,K
, i.e.,
y_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)
iff for some negative type
T
, we have
y_{\alpha}{:}T\in\Delta
or for some positive type
T
, we have
y_{\alpha}{:}T\in K
.

Definition 19
.

\mathit{dom}(\Delta)
is a set defined inductively as:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathit{dom}(\Delta,y_{\alpha}:A)&=&\mathit{dom}(\Delta)\cup\{y\}\\
\mathit{dom}(\cdot)&=&\emptyset\end{array}

Definition 20

(Open configuration transitions)
.

We provide some notations used in the logical relation and proofs.

•

The notation
\mapsto^{*}
refers to taking none or many steps with
\mapsto
. The notation
\mapsto^{j}
refers to taking
j
steps with
\mapsto
.

•

We write
\mathcal{D}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
stating that
\mathcal{D}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is ready to send along
\Upsilon
.

•

We write
\mathcal{D}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon;\Theta}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
stating that
\mathcal{D}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is ready to send along
\Upsilon
and ready to receive along
\Theta
.

\begin{array}[]{ll}(r_{1})&({\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\cdot\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}1\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;y^{\alpha}}^{m+1}\\
&\;\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\cdot\Vdash y_{\alpha})\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\\[2.0pt]
(r_{2})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle)\rrbracket_{\cdot;y^{\alpha}}^{m+1}\\
&\;\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}:\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\exists k_{1},k_{2}\in I.\,\,(c\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow k_{1}=k_{2})\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\,\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2})\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\,\mathsf{and}\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k_{1}}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\\[2.0pt]
(r_{3})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{y^{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\\
&\;\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{then}\forall k_{1},k_{2}\in I.\mathsf{if}\,(c\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow k_{1}=k_{2})\,\mathsf{then}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1}),\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k_{1}}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\\[2.0pt]
(r_{4})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;y^{\alpha}}^{m+1}\\
&\;\mathsf{iff}(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\,\exists x_{\beta}.\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\\[2.0pt]
(r_{5})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{y^{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\\
&\;\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\,\forall x_{\beta}{\not\in}\mathit{dom}(\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle).\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle});\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\end{array}

Figure 18:
Value interpretation of logical relation for
right
communications.

\begin{array}[]{ll}(l_{1})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}1\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y^{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\\

&\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K)\,\mathsf{and}\,\exists k_{1},k_{2}\in I.(c\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow k_{1}=k_{2})\,\mathsf{and}\\

&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta,y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k_{1}}{\langle c,e\rangle}\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m}\\[4.0pt]
(l_{4})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y^{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\\
&\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K)\,\mathsf{and}\forall x_{\beta}{\not\in}\mathit{dom}(\Delta,y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle,K).\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle},y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle},y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle,y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m}\\[4.0pt]
(l_{5})&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime},y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;y^{\alpha}}^{m+1}\\
&\mathsf{iff}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime},y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K)\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\;\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 17.00024pt(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime},y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m}\end{array}

Value interpretation of logical relation for
left
communications.

\begin{array}[]{lcl}({\mathcal{B}_{1}},{\mathcal{B}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}&\mathsf{iff}&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta\Vdash K)\,\mathsf{and}\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\,\Theta_{1},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\;\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\;\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\,\mathsf{then}\\
&&\exists\,\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\mathsf{\,such\,that\,}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\;\mathsf{and}\;\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
&&\forall\,y_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,y_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\[2.0pt]
&&\forall\,y_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,y_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\\[4.0pt]
({\mathcal{B}_{1}},{\mathcal{B}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{0}&\mathsf{iff}&(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi_{0}}(\Delta\Vdash K)\end{array}

Figure 20:
Term interpretation of logical relation.

Next, we define the projection function
{\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}}\xi
to build the integrity interface, e.g.,
\Delta{\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}}\xi
projects out the channels in
\Delta
that have lower or incomparable integrity than
\xi
.

Definition 21

(Typing context projections -integrity)
.

Downward projection on linear contexts and
K
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lclc}\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&\mathsf{if}\;e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\cdot\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\cdot&\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\sqsubseteq\xi\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\_{:}1\langle\top,\top\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\end{array}

Definition 22

(Low integrity provider and Low integrity client)
.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\cdot\,\,\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\cdot)\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\mathcal{T}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
&&\,\,\mathcal{T}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\cdot\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle),\mathbf{or}\\
&&e\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\\[10.0pt]
\mathcal{T}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{T}\in\mathsf{Tree}(x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash\_:1\langle\top,\top\rangle),\mathbf{or}\\
&&e\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{T}=\cdot\end{array}

Two open configurations are in the integrity relation if they are related by the term interpretation for every possible pair of substitutions for their low integrity channels and any number of observations:

Definition 23

(Equivalence of trees by the logical relation upto the observer level)
.

We define the relation

(\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\equiv^{\Psi_{0}}_{\xi}(\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)\,\,\mathsf{as}

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{1}}\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{2}}\Vdash y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=K\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}).\,\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}).\\
