\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle).\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\\[1.0pt]
\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}.\end{array}

13
Fundamental theorem

Theorem 24

(Fundamental Theorem)
.

For all security levels
\xi
, and

{\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle},\quad\mathsf{and}\quad{\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle}

with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
,
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi
, and
u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi
we have

(\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\equiv^{\Psi}_{\xi}(\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).

Proof.

Our goal is to show:

For all well-typed
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, i.e.,
{\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}
and
{\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle}
,
with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta
, and
u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=K
we have

\forall\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}).\,\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).

\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{1}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{1}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}.

Without loss of generality, we only prove one part of this symmetric relation, i.e.:

For all well-typed
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, i.e.,
{\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}
and
{\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle}
,
with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta
, and
u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=K
we have

\forall\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}).\,\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).

\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{1}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m},\,

The goal is equivalent to:

(\star_{1})\,\,
For all
m
, for all
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
that are IFC-typed, i.e.,
{\Psi;\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}
and
{\Psi;\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle}
, with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow\xi=\Delta
, and
u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow\xi=v_{\beta}{:}T_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow\xi=K
we have

\forall\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}).\,\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}[{c_{1}}]).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}[{c_{2}}]).

(\mathcal{B}_{1}{\mathcal{D}}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}

First, we show that it is enough to prove the following goal.

(\star_{2})\,\,
For all
m
, for all
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
that are well-typed, i.e.,
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}::K}
and
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}::K}
, with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
\Delta\Downarrow\xi=\Delta
, and
K\Downarrow\xi=K
we have

({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}},{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}

We need to show that from
\star_{1}
, we get
\star_{2}
:

Apply a for all introduction on
\star_{2}
to get an arbitrary number
m
, configurations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and high providers
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}
and high clients
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
satisfying the given assumptions.

We define
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}
. Note that these two configurations are both well-typed, and also we have

\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}::K
and
\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}::K
. We also know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\Downarrow\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\Downarrow\xi
. Observe that all relevant and semi-relevant nodes occur in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, for which by assumption we know
\mathcal{D}_{1}\Downarrow\xi=\mathcal{D}_{2}\Downarrow\xi
. Now we can apply
\star_{2}
to get what we want.

It remains to prove
(\star_{2})
:

(\star_{2})\,\,
For all
m
, for all
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
that are IFC-typed, i.e.,
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::K}
and
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::K}
, with
\mathcal{D}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\mathcal{D}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
, and
\Delta\Downarrow\xi=\Delta
, and
K\Downarrow\xi=K
we have

({\mathcal{D}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}

The proof is by induction on the index
m
.

Base case.
m=0
.
We consider arbitrary configurations (
\forall I
on the goal). By the configuration typing, we know that

({\mathcal{D}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}_{2}})\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta}\Vdash{K}),

which is enough to complete the proof.

Inductive case.
m=m^{\prime}+1
.

(\mathcal{B}_{1}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{B}_{2}{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta}\Vdash{K}).

Consider an arbitrary
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
such that
{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon;\Theta}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}
.
By Preservation we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
is IFC-typed for the same interface
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}::K}

By
Lem.

14
, for some
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, we get
{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}}
, such that
{\Psi;\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}::K}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\Downarrow\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\Downarrow\xi
.

We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}\;(\dagger_{1})\;\forall u^{\prime}_{\delta}\in\mathbf{Out}({\Delta}\Vdash{K}).\,\mathbf{if}\,u^{\prime}_{\delta}\in\Upsilon.\,\mathbf{then}\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m}\;\mathbf{and}\\
\;(\dagger_{2})\;\forall u^{\prime}_{\delta}\in\mathbf{In}({\Delta}\Vdash{K}).\,\mathbf{if}\,u^{\prime}_{\delta}\in\Theta.\,\mathsf{then}\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket_{u^{\prime}_{\delta};\cdot}^{m}\end{array}

We prove both
\dagger_{1}
, and
\dagger_{2}
. We consider an arbitrary
u^{\prime}_{\delta}
and provide a case analysis based on its type. If there is no such
u^{\prime}_{\delta}
in the specified set, then the statements trivially holds. There might be a channel in both of these sets, if and only if
\mathcal{D}_{1}=\cdot
.

Proof of
\dagger_{1}
.

Consider an arbitrary channel
u^{\prime}_{\delta}{:}T\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)
and assume
u^{\prime}_{\delta}{:}T\in\Upsilon
. We consider different cases based on the session type
T
.

Case 1.

u^{\prime}{:}T\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=K=u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle=u_{\alpha}{:}1\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,u^{c_{1}}_{\alpha})
. By the typing rules we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\cdot
, and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}=\cdot
, and
\Delta=\cdot
.
Note that by the definition of relevancy,
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and the tree invariant, all nodes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are either relevant or semi-relevant.
As a result,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}),
and by the typing rules
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\cdot
.

By row
r_{1}
in the definition, we get

\dagger_{1}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\cdot\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}1[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m},

Case 2.

\begin{array}[]{l}u^{\prime}{:}T[\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]=K=u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]=u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle],\,\mathsf{and}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.k)\end{array}

By the assumption of the theorem, every node in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
are either relevant or semi-relevant. In particular,
\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.k)
is either relevant or semi-relavant.

Subcase 1.

\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.k)
is relevant.

We have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.k).

Removing
\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k;u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha})
from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
does not change relevancy of the remaining configuration when
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
:

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{{}\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
r_{2}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k);\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k))\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.k)
is semi-relevant, i.e.
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, but
c_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. We get
c_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and by the typing rules, we know
\forall i,j\in I.\,A_{i}=A_{j}=A^{\prime}
, i.e.
u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in I}[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]

By the definition, we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}.j),
for some
j\in I
.
Removing
\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k)
and
\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.j)
from
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, respectively, does not change relevancy of the remaining configuration when
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
:

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{{}\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}A^{\prime}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
r_{2}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k);\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k))\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 3.

\begin{array}[]{l}u^{\prime}{:}T[\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]=K=u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]=u_{\alpha}{:}(A\otimes B)[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle],\,\mathsf{and}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}),\,\mathsf{where}\\
\Delta=\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}$ and $\Psi;\Lambda_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}::(x_{\beta}{:}A[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle])\end{array}

By assumption of the theorem, we know every node in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
is either relevant or semi-relevant. In particular,
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{c_{1}}\,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle})
is either relevant or semi-relevant.
In both cases,

{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}\,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle})}

such that
\Psi;\Lambda_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{2}::(x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
.Moreover, channels
x_{\beta}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}
and
u_{\alpha+1}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}
are relevant and we get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
and

\mathcal{T}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{T}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

In the case where
x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\in\Delta
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{i}=\cdot
and
\Lambda_{2}=x_{\beta}{:}A[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]
.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star^{\prime}}&(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Lambda_{2}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star^{\prime\prime}}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Lambda_{1}\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}B[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
r_{4}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}\,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle});\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}\,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}))\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B[\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle]\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 4.

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle,\,\mathsf{and}\\
u^{\prime}{:}T^{\prime}\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=x_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\end{array}

By the assumption of the theorem, we know that
\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)
is either relevant or semi-relevant.

Subcase 1.

\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)
is relevant.

By definition,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
:
e\sqsubseteq\xi
and channel
u^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
is relevant in
\mathcal{D}_{i}
and no relevancy changes in the configurations after removing the negative message.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By line (
l_{3}
) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}[\langle c,e\rangle]\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)
is semi-relevant, i.e.
e\sqsubseteq\xi
and
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. We get
c\not\sqsubseteq e
. By inversion on the typing rules, we get
\forall i,j\in L.A_{i}=A_{j}=A^{\prime}
, i.e.,
x_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell:A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle
.

By definition,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.j)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2},
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
:
e\sqsubseteq\xi
and channel
u^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
is relevant in
\mathcal{D}_{i}
and no relevancy changes in the configurations after removing the negative message.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}{:}A^{\prime}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
l_{3}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.j)\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in L}[\langle c,e\rangle]\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}_{\delta}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 5.

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}(A\multimap B)[\langle c,e\rangle],\,\mathsf{and}\\
u^{\prime}{:}T^{\prime}\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=x_{\gamma}{:}(A\multimap B)[\langle c,e\rangle],\,\mathsf{and}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{such\,that}\\
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}::y_{\delta}{:}A[\langle c,e\rangle]\,\mathsf{and}\\
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}{:}B[\langle c,e\rangle]\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}::K.\end{array}

The message
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
is either relevant or semi-relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
.

In both cases,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}),
such that
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{2}::y_{\delta}{:}A[c]
and
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}{:}B[c]\Vdash\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}::K
.

By typing configuration, we have
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{A}_{i}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{A}_{i}::\Delta^{\prime}
and
\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{i}::\Delta^{\prime\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}(A\multimap B)[\langle c,e\rangle]
.

By assumption we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
. By definition of relevancy and the tree invariant, we know that every node in the tree
\mathcal{T}_{1}
is either relevant or semi-relevant in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and thus
\mathcal{T}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{T}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
. Removing these from both configurations does not change relevancy of the rest of the configuration since
w^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\eta}
will remain relevant, and the relevancy of the message’s parent does not change:
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star\,}&(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash y_{\delta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}\,\mathsf{and}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star^{\prime}\,}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
l_{5}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\delta}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}_{1},x_{\gamma}{:}A\multimap B[\langle c,e\rangle]\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;u^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Proof of
\dagger_{2}
.

Consider an arbitrary channel
u^{\prime}:T[\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle]\in\Theta_{1}\cap\Theta_{2}
. We consider cases based on the type of this channel, and whether there is a message or a forwarding process ready to be executed along it in the first configuration; (it cannot be both message and forward).

Case 1.

\begin{array}[]{l}u^{\prime}{:}T\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=K=u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle=u_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\end{array}

We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.

c_{1},e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
.

We use row
r_{3}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.

From
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
and
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we get

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k){\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k){\Downarrow}\xi.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the index
m^{\prime}<m
.
By induction hypothesis

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k);\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By line (
r_{3}
) in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket_{u^{\prime}_{\delta};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
and
c_{1}\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.

By inversion on the typing rules, we know that
\forall i,j\in I.\,A_{i}=A_{j}=A^{\prime}
.

We use
(r_{3})
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.

From
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi
and
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we get

\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k){\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.j){\Downarrow}\xi.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the index
m^{\prime}<m
.
By induction hypothesis

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.k);\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}.j))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}A^{\prime}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
r_{3}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell:A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket_{u^{\prime}_{\delta};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 2.

\begin{array}[]{l}u^{\prime}_{\delta}{:}T\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=K=u_{\alpha}{:}T_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle=u_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\end{array}

We use row
r_{5}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.

By assumption and
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, we know that every node in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
is either relevant or semi-relevant. Adding a negative message along
e_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
as the root does not change their relevancy:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\beta}\,u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\beta}\,u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}){\Downarrow}\xi\end{array}

We apply the induction hypothesis on
m^{\prime}<m
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\beta}\,u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha});\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\beta}\,u^{\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle}_{\alpha}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
r_{5}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\rrbracket_{u^{\prime};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 3.

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}1\langle c,e\rangle.\end{array}

We use row
l_{1}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.
Assume
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
.

We first briefly explain why the invariant of induction holds after we bring the closing message inside
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
and remove the channel
x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle}
from
\Delta
, i.e.

\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

If the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
is relevant or semi-relevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
before bringing the message inside then by the assumption of the theorem, there is a related node as the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
.

•

If after adding the message to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
the parent still remains relevant or semi-relevant, it means that it has at least another relevant channel other than
x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}
; that channel also exists in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and will be relevant after adding the message to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
.

•

If after adding the message, the parent in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
becomes irrelevant, it means that it does not have a relevant path to any other channel in
\Delta^{\prime}
and
K
.
In this case, if the the parent in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
does not become irrelevant, it means that it has a relevant path to another channel in
\Delta^{\prime}
and
K
. But by the assumption of theorem, this only is possible if this path goes over a dead-end node in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, meaning that the parent is dead-end.
The same argument holds for any other node that becomes irrelevant because of adding the closing message to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i}
.

If the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
is irrelevant in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
before bringing the message inside then by the assumption of the theorem, the the parent of
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
has to be either irrelevant or a dead-end node before bringing in the message; the parent remains irrelevant or dead-end after bringing the message inside too.

Now that the invariant holds, we can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
:

{\star}\;(\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.

By row
l_{1}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
,

\dagger_{2}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}1\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{u^{\prime};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}

Case 4.

\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\,\mathsf{and}\,u^{\prime}{:}T^{\prime}\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}[\langle c,e\rangle]\end{array}

We consider two subcases:

Subcase 1.

c,e\sqsubseteq\xi
.
We use line (
l_{2}
) in definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.

We have

\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

The channel
u^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}

is relevant and the positive messages
\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)
in both configurations are relevant only if their parents are either relevant or semi-relevant. If the parent is either relevant or semi-relevant in the first configuration, the parent is relevant or semi-relevant in the second configuration too, and the messages are both relevant.
If the parent is irrelevant in the first configuration, the parent has to be either irrelevant or a dead-end node in the second configuration; the message in the first configuration is irrelevant and in the second configuration is either irrelevant or dead-end.
Adding the message does not change relevancy of any other process.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star\;}&(\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}:A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
l_{2}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{u^{\prime}_{\delta};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Subcase 2.

e\sqsubseteq\xi
and
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
.
We know
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, and thus
\forall i,j\in L.\,A_{i}=A_{j}=A^{\prime}
.

We use row
l_{2}
in definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove the result.

We have

\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.j)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.

The channel
u^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}

is relevant and the positive messages
\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)
in both configurations are semi-relevant only if their parents are either relevant or semi-relevant.
If the parent is either relevant or semi-relevant in the first configuration, the parent is relevant or semi-relevant in the second configuration too, and the messages are both semi-relevant.
If the parent is irrelevant in the first configuration, the parent has to be either irrelevant or a dead-end node in the second configuration; the message in the first configuration is irrelevant and in the second configuration is either irrelevant or dead-end.
Adding the message does not change relevancy of any other process.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star\;}&(\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.j))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},u_{\alpha+1}:A^{\prime}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
l_{2}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell:A^{\prime}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{u^{\prime};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Case 5.

\begin{array}[]{ll}\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}(A\otimes B)\langle c,e\rangle\,\mathsf{and}\,u^{\prime}_{\delta}{:}T^{\prime}\langle c^{\prime},e^{\prime}\rangle=x_{\gamma}{:}(A\otimes B)\langle c,e\rangle\end{array}

We use line
l_{4}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
to prove this case.

We have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,y_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}{\Downarrow}\xi=_{\xi}\\
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,y_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}{\Downarrow}\xi.\end{array}

Channels
u^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha+1}
and
y^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\alpha}

are relevant and in both configurations.

The positive messages
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle})
are (semi-)relevant only if their parents are either relevant or semi-relevant.
If the parent is either relevant or semi-relevant in the first configuration, the parent is relevant or semi-relevant in the second configuration too, and the messages are both (semi-)relevant.
If the parent is irrelevant in the first configuration, the parent has to be either irrelevant or a dead-end node in the second configuration; the message in the first configuration is irrelevant and in the second configuration is either irrelevant or dead-end.
Adding the message does not change relevancy of any other process.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
m^{\prime}<m
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\star}&(\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\delta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},y_{\delta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle,u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}}.\end{array}

By row
l_{4}
in the definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}
:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},x_{\gamma}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K\rrbracket_{u^{\prime}_{\delta};\cdot}^{m^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

∎

14
Useful properties of the logical relation

Lemma 25

(Building minimal sending configuration)
.

Consider
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::K
, a set of channels
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Delta,K
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
for some
\Upsilon_{2}\supseteq\Upsilon_{1}
. There exists a set
\Upsilon
and a configuration
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
such that
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and
\forall\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2},\Upsilon_{3}\supseteq\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{3}}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\,\mathsf{then}\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{3}}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}
. We call
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and
\Upsilon
the minimal sending configuration and the minimal sending set with respect to
\Upsilon_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, respectively.

Proof.

A property of session-typed processes. Refer to
[
7
]
for the proof and the algorithm.
∎

Lemma 26

(Backward closure on the second run)
.

The second run enjoys backward closure:

1.

If
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}

and for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
then

(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
.

2.

If
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}^{k+1}
and for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
sending along channel
y_{\alpha}
,
then

(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}^{k+1}.

3.

If
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}
and for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\Delta\Vdash K)
, we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, then

(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}.

Proof.

We prove the first statement separately and then use it to prove the second and third statements.

1.

If
k=0
, the proof is trivial. Consider
k=m+1
.
By assumption, we have
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.
By the term interpretation
20
of the logical relation we know

\begin{array}[]{l}(\star)\,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Consider
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, for which by the assumption we have
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
. Our goal is to prove
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.
We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger)\,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

With a
\forall
-Introduction, an
\mathsf{if}
-Introduction on the goal followed by a
\forall
-Elimination and an
\mathsf{if}
-Elimination on the assumption, we get the assumption

\begin{array}[]{l}(\star^{\prime})\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

and the goal

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger^{\prime})\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

We apply an
\exists
-Elimination on the assumption to get
\Upsilon_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
that satisfies the conditions and use the same
\Upsilon_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
to instantiate the existential quantifier in the goal. Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, we get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, and the proof is complete.

2.

We proceed the proof by cases on the row of the logical relation that makes the assumption
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}^{k+1}
correct.
Here we only consider two interesting cases, the proof of other cases is similar.

Row
r_{2}
.

By the conditions of this row, we know that
K=y_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle
.

We have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k)\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j)\,\mathsf{and}\\
\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

where
j=k
if
c\sqsubseteq\xi
.

These are also the statements that we need to prove when replacing
\mathcal{D}_{2}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
.
By the assumption that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
also sends along
y
, uniqueness of channels, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
we get

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j)

Moreover,
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
.
Note that if
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
c\not\sqsubseteq e
, then
A_{k}=A_{j}
.

Now we can apply the the first item of this lemma on
\dagger_{1}
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger^{\prime\prime}_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

and the proof of this subcase is complete.

Row
r_{4}
.

By the conditions of this row, we know that
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, and
K=y_{\alpha}{:}A\otimes B\langle c,e\rangle
.

We have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\mathcal{D}_{1}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\mathcal{D}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send},x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)\,\mathsf{and}\\
\dagger_{1}&(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\dagger_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

These are also the statements that we need to prove when replacing
\mathcal{D}_{2}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
.
By the assumption that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
also sends along
y
, uniqueness of channels, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}
we get

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{1}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send},x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\,\mathsf{for}\,\mathcal{T}^{1}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)

Moreover, since
\mathcal{T}^{1}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}
are disjoint sub-trees and cannot communicate with each other internally, we have

\mathcal{T}^{1}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{T}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
.

Now we can apply the the first item of this lemma on
\dagger_{1}
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger^{\prime}_{1}&(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}^{1}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime\prime}\Vdash x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}.\end{array}

Similarly we apply the first item of this lemma on
\dagger_{2}
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger^{\prime\prime}_{2}&(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{1}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash u_{\delta}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

and the proof of this subcase is complete.

3.

The proof is by considering the row of the logical relation that ensures the assumption
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{y_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k}
. Here we only consider two interesting cases, the proof of other cases is similar.

Row
r_{3}
.

By the conditions of this row, we know that
K=y_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle
.
We have

\begin{array}[]{l}(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in I}\langle c,e\rangle)\end{array}

Moreover, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\forall k,j\in I\,\mathsf{where}\,k=j\,\mathsf{if}\,c\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{then}\\
\dagger&(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k);\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

These are also the statements that we need to prove when replacing
\mathcal{D}_{2}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. The first tree statements are straightforward by type preservation.

By assumption,

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j)\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j).

Note that if
c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
c\not\sqsubseteq e
, and
A_{k}=A_{j}
.

We can apply the first item of this lemma on
\dagger
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger^{\prime}&(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k);\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.j))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

which completes the proof of this case.

Row
r_{5}
.

By the conditions of this row, we know that
K=y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle
.
We have

\begin{array}[]{l}(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathsf{Tree}_{\Psi}(\Delta\Vdash y_{\alpha}{:}A\multimap B\langle c,e\rangle)\end{array}

Moreover, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\forall x_{\beta}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Delta,y_{\alpha+1}:A_{k}\langle c,e\rangle).\\
\dagger&(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle});\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

These are also the statements that we need to prove when replacing
\mathcal{D}_{2}
with
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. The first tree statements are straightforward by type preservation.

By assumption,

\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle})\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}).

We can apply the first item of this lemma on
\dagger
to get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\dagger^{\prime}&(\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle});\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}x_{\beta}^{\langle c,e\rangle}\,y_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi}\llbracket\Delta,x_{\beta}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash y_{\alpha+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k}\end{array}

which completes the proof of this case.

Rows
l_{1}
,
l_{2}
, and
l_{4}
.

Similar to the previous cases.

∎

Lemma 27

(Forward closure on the first run)
.

Consider
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
. We have

(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}.

Proof.

If
k=0
the proof is trivial. Consider
k=m+1
.
By assumption, we have
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.

By the term interpretation (Figure
20
) of the logical relation we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\star\,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Consider
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
, for which by the assumption we have
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
. Our goal is to prove
(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.
We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

With a
\forall
-Introduction and an
\mathsf{if}
-Introduction on the goal, we assume
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
. By assumption of
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
we get
\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
. We use this to apply
\forall
-Elimination and
\mathsf{if}
- Elimination on the assumption, and get

\begin{array}[]{l}\star^{\prime}\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Which exactly matches our goal and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 28

(Forward closure on the second run with some specific conditions)
.

Consider
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
such that if
\mathcal{D}_{1}
sends along the set
\Upsilon_{1}
, we have
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon
and also
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is the minimal configuration built by
Lem.

25
given the set
\Upsilon_{1}
and configuration
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. We have

(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}.

Proof.

If
k=0
the proof is trivial. Consider
k=m+1
.
By assumption, we have
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.
By the term interpretation (Figure
20
) of the logical relation we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\star\,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Consider
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, for which by the assumption we have
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. Our goal is to prove
(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m+1}
.
We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

With an
\mathsf{if}
-Introduction on the goal followed by an
\mathsf{if}
- Elimination on the assumption, we get the assumption

\begin{array}[]{l}\star^{\prime}\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

and the goal

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger^{\prime}\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,x(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

We apply an
\exists
-Elimination on the assumption to get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
that satisfies the conditions, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}
. We use the same
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
to instantiate the existential quantifier in the goal, we need to show that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is the minimal configuration built for
\Upsilon_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}
, by Lemma
25
we get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 29

(Moving existential over universal quantifier)
.

if we have

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger)\,\,\forall m.\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

then

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,k.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{k+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall k.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}.\end{array}

Proof.

First put
m=1
to apply
\forall\,\mathbf{E}.
on the assumption (instantiating
\forall\,m
only). We get as an assumption

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger^{\prime})\,\,\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{0+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{0+1}.\end{array}

Next, apply a
\forall\mathbf{I}.
and
\mathbf{if}\,\mathbf{I}.
on the goal followed by a corresponding
\forall\mathbf{E}.
and
\mathbf{if}\,\mathbf{E}.
on the assumption
(\dagger^{\prime})
. Now apply an
\exists\mathbf{E}.
on the assumption
(\dagger^{\prime})
to get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}
. Given
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, by
Lem.

25
, we can build the minimal
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon
. Moreover, we know that for every
\mathcal{D}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{3}}}\mathcal{D}
and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{3}
, we get
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{3}}}\mathcal{D}
.

We use this minimal
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
to instantiate the existential (
\exists\,\mathbf{I}.
) in the goal, and use
\forall\,\mathbf{I}.
on the goal. In particular, we instantiate
k
with an arbitrary natural number.
The goals are:

\begin{array}[]{l}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{k+1}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}.\end{array}

Next, we instantiate the
\forall
quantifier in the original assumption (
\dagger
) once again, this time with
m=k
followed by a
\forall\mathbf{E}
, and
\mathbf{if}\mathbf{E}
instantiating the quantifiers with similar
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\Upsilon_{1}
, and
\Theta_{1}
as the first time. We get as an assumption:

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger^{\prime\prime})\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{k+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\alpha}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\alpha}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}.\end{array}

Next, apply
\exists\,\mathbf{I}.
to get a
\Upsilon^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
that satisfies the conditions, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
and
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon^{\prime}
. Instantiate
x_{\alpha}
as those chosen for the goal. We have as assumptions:

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger^{\prime\prime\prime})\,\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\alpha}}^{k+1}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,({\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\alpha};\cdot}^{k+1}.\end{array}

Since
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is the minimal configuration built for
\Upsilon_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. We can apply the backward closure results of
Lem.

26
to get the goal from the assumptions
(\dagger^{\prime\prime\prime})
, and this completes the proof.

∎

Lemma 30

(Compositionlaity)
.

Consider
\Delta,u_{\alpha}\langle c,e\rangle{:}T\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{i}::K
and
\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{i}::u_{\alpha}\langle c,e\rangle{:}T
.

\begin{array}[]{l}(\dagger_{1})\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta,u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}{:}T\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m}\,\mathsf{and}(\dagger_{2})\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{T}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime}\Vdash u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}{:}T\rrbracket^{m}\,\mathsf{iff}\\
\dagger\,\forall k.\,(\mathcal{T}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{T}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\prime},\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}.\end{array}

Proof.

The left to right direction is a corollary of Lemma
31
in which we compose multiple configurations instead of just two.
For the right to left direction, we put
\mathcal{T}_{i}=\cdot
, and
\Delta^{\prime}=u_{\alpha}{:}T
, and the rest of the proof is straightforward.
∎

Lemma 31

(General Compositionality)
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, and index set
I
consider well-typed tree-shaped configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}

such that
n\in I
and their compositions form well-typed tree-shaped configurations
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{i}::K
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{i}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}\}_{n\in I}
. If for all
n\in I
, we have

\dagger_{n}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}
then
\dagger\,\forall k.\,(\mathcal{D}_{1};\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
.

Proof.

Our goal is to prove the following:

For all index set
I
and all session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
with
n\in I
such that

\dagger_{1}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}
we have
\dagger\,\,\forall k.\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
.

This is equivalent to the following statement which we prove:

For all natural numbers,
k
, and for all index set
I
, and for all session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
such that

\dagger_{n}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}
we have
\dagger^{\prime}\,\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k}
.

We proceed the proof by an induction on k.

Base case (
k=0
).
The proof is straightforward, since by the definition of the logical relation for session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
with
\Delta\Vdash\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}\}_{n\in I}::K
, we have
(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{0}
.

Inductive case (
k=k^{\prime}+1
).

Our goal is to prove the following:

For all index set
I
, and for all session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
where
n\in I
such that

\dagger_{n}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}
we have
\dagger^{\prime}\,\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket^{k^{\prime}+1}

where
\dagger^{\prime}
is defined in the term interpretation (Figure
20
) of the logical relation as

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,{\Upsilon_{1}},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\forall\,j\in\mathbb{N}.\,\mathbf{if}\,{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}}\mapsto^{{j}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Again, we rewrite the above goal as an equivalent statement as follows:

For all natural numbers
j
, for all
I
, and for all session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
with
n\in I
such that

\dagger_{n}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}
we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\,\mathbf{if}\,{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}}\mapsto^{{j}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

We proceed the proof by a nested induction on
j
.

Base case (
j=0
)
. Consider an arbitrary index set
I
and an arbitrary session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
that satisfy the conditions
\dagger_{n}
. We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathbf{if}\,{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}}\mapsto^{{0}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Consider an arbitrary
\Upsilon_{1}
,
\Theta_{1}
, ans
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
, and apply
\mathbf{If}\,\mathbf{I}.
on the goal. By the assumption
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{0}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}
we know that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}
, and
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}
sends along
\Upsilon_{1}
and receives along
\Theta_{1}
. Our goal is to show the following:

\begin{array}[]{l}\star\,\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

By the definition of the logical relation, and from assumptions
\dagger_{n}
for
n\in I
we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger^{\prime}_{n}\,\forall m.\forall\Upsilon_{n},\Theta_{n},\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{n};\Theta_{n}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{n_{2}},\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{n_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon_{n_{2}}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

By
Lem.

29
, we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger^{\prime\prime}_{n}\,\,\forall\Upsilon_{n},\Theta_{n},\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1}.\,\mathsf{if}\,\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{n};\Theta_{n}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{n_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{n_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon_{n_{2}}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

We instantiate the forall quantifier in
\dagger^{\prime\prime}_{n}
by
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}
and the sets
\Upsilon_{n}
and
\Theta_{n}
along which
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}
sends and receives. Note that by definition we have
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\bigcup\{\Upsilon_{n}\}_{n\in I}
and
\Theta_{1}\subseteq\bigcup\{\Theta_{n}\}_{n\in I}
. We get:

\begin{array}[]{l}\exists\Upsilon_{n_{2}},\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{n_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

By existential elimination, for all
n\in I
, we get a
\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}
and
\Upsilon_{n_{2}}
such that
