\mathcal{B}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{n_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}
, and
\Upsilon_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon_{n_{2}}
and

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger^{\prime\prime\prime}_{n}\,\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Note that by definition
\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in I}\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n})
and
\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K)\subseteq\bigcup_{n\in I}\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n})
.

We apply
Lem.

25
to get the minimal sending configurations
\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}
and set
\Upsilon^{\prime}_{n}
for each given
\Upsilon_{n}
and
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}
. We get
\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon^{\prime}_{n}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}
. Since these configurations are minimal, for all
n\in I
, we have
\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{n_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}
.

Since
\Upsilon_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon^{\prime}_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon_{n_{2}}
, we can apply the backward closure (
Lem.

26
) on
\dagger^{\prime\prime\prime}_{n}
to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\dagger^{4}_{n}\,\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}.\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}.\end{array}

Moreover, we apply forward closure on the second run
Lem.

28
on assumptions
\dagger_{n}
to get for all
n\in I
:

\circ_{n}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}

We can apply the forward closure on the second run since the conditions of
Lem.

28
are satisfied, i.e.
\Upsilon_{n}\subseteq\Upsilon^{\prime}_{n}
and
\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}
is the minimal sending configuration with respect to
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}
and
\Upsilon_{n}
.

We build
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
to be
\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}
. We have
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}
with
\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}
, and
\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{3}}}\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}
with
\Upsilon_{2}\subseteq\Upsilon_{3}
.
We, then instantiate the existential quantifier in the goal (
\star
) with
\Upsilon_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
that we built for which we know
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{*_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
. We need to show

\begin{array}[]{l}\star^{\prime}\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Part 1.
Consider arbitrary
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K)
and assume
x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}
. By the structure of the configurations, for some
n\in I
,
x_{\gamma}\in\Delta^{n},K^{n}
and thus
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n})
and
x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{n}
. The goal is to prove

\star_{1}\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}

Part 2.

Consider arbitrary
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K)
and assume
x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}
. By the structure of the configurations, for some
n\in I
,
x_{\gamma}\in\Delta^{n},K^{n}
and thus
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n})
and
x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{n}
. The goal is to prove

\star_{2}\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I};\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}

In both parts, we continue the proof by considering the type of
x_{\gamma}
. The type of

determines whether we need to prove

or
Part 2
. We provide the detailed proof for two interesting cases, the proof of the rest of cases is similar. Also see the proof of similar lemma in .

Subcase 1.

x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\in K
. This case corresponds to
Part 1.
of the goal in which we have
x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle)
and
x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}
. By the structure of the configuration, there exists a tree
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\kappa}
that provides the root channel
K=K^{\kappa}=x_{\gamma}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle
. We use assumption
\dagger^{4}_{n}
for that specific channel (
\dagger^{4}_{\kappa}
), we have

\dagger^{5}_{\kappa}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash K^{\kappa}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}

First, instantiate the forall quantifier with

.
By
Row
r_{2}
.
of the logical relation, we have
\Delta^{\kappa}=\Delta^{\kappa}_{1},\Delta^{\kappa}_{2}
and for some

, we have:

\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})
and
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{msg}(x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2}))
, such that if

we have

. Moreover, by well-typedness we know that

.

We want to prove

\circ^{\prime}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}.

Consider an arbitrary
m
given by
\forall\mathbf{I}
on the goal
\circ^{\prime}
. Once again, instantiate the quantifier in

, this time with the arbitrary

. Again, we get

and for some

\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x_{\gamma}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})

Moreover,

(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m}.

Since the namings in the configuration is unique, we get the above for the same

as we got in the case of

is complete.

From this we can prove

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{D}_{1}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I\&n\neq\kappa}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{1})\,\mathsf{and}\\
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I\&n\neq\kappa}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{2})\end{array}

where
k_{1}=k_{2}
if
c\sqsubseteq\xi
.
First, observe that by the structure of the configuration, there is no tree
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{n}
or
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{n^{\prime\prime}}
using

We can break down the resources

In other words, we have

and

\begin{array}[]{llll}(i)&\Delta_{1}\Vdash\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}::\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{1}&\;\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1},\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}::x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle&\;\Delta_{1},\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\Vdash\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}::x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\\
&\Delta_{1}\Vdash\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}::\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{1}&\;\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1},\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}::x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle&\;\Delta_{1},\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\Vdash\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}::x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\end{array}

We also have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\circ^{\prime\prime\prime}&\mathcal{D}_{1}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I\&n\neq\kappa}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{1})=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{1})\,\mathsf{and}\\
&\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I\&n\neq\kappa}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{2})=\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}_{\gamma}.k_{2})\end{array}

Recall that earlier we proved

\circ^{\prime}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{m},

and we also have for all

\circ_{2}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index
k^{\prime}
, (i),
\circ^{\prime\prime}
, and
\circ_{2}
for those trees indexed in

to get

\circ_{3}\,(\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1};\{\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta,\Delta^{\kappa^{\prime}}\Vdash x_{\gamma+1}{:}B\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket^{k^{\prime}}.

.
of the logical relation, this is enough to establish the goal

Subcase 2.

\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\in\Delta

.

Recall that for all

we also have

\circ_{2}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the smaller index

,
\circ_{2}
, and
\dagger^{5}_{\kappa}
to get for all

, such that

By

of the logical relation, this is enough to establish the goal.

Inductive case (
j=j^{\prime}+1
).

Consider an arbitrary index set
I
and an arbitrary session-typed configurations
\Delta^{n}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{n}_{i}::K^{n}
that satisfy the conditions
\dagger_{n}
.
We need to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathbf{if}\,{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}}\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}+1}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

We apply a
\forall\mathbf{I}.
and
\mathbf{if}\,\mathbf{I}.
on the goal: consider an arbitrary
\Upsilon_{1}
,
\Theta_{1}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and the first step of
{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}}\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}+1}_{\Upsilon_{1}};\Theta_{1}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}
. There are two cases to consider:

Case 1. the first step is an internal step but not a communication between the sub-trees.

Without loss of generality, let’s assume that the communication is internal to
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}
for some
\kappa\in I
and all other trees
\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}
for
n\neq\kappa
remain intact, i.e. we have
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}
and

\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}.

By forward closure (
Lem.

27
) on the assumption
\dagger_{\kappa}
(i.e.,
\dagger_{n}
for
n=\kappa
), we get

\dagger^{\prime}_{\kappa}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash K^{\kappa}\rrbracket^{m}.

Recall that by
\dagger_{n}
, we also have for
n\neq\kappa

\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{n}\Vdash K^{n}\rrbracket^{m}.

We can apply the induction hypothesis on the number of steps
j^{\prime}
to get:

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\forall\,{\Upsilon_{1}}.\,\mathbf{if}\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\,\mathsf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

Since
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}},

we can prove the goal:

\begin{array}[]{l}\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

which completes the proof of this case.

Case 2. the first step is a communication between two sub-configurations.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the communication is between trees indexed by
\kappa,\lambda\in I
, i.e.,
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}
offers a resource
u_{\alpha}{:}T
and
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}
uses the resource, and there is a message available along
u_{\alpha}
that is received in the first step.
The proof proceeds by case analysis on type of
u_{\alpha}^{c}{:}T
. We provide the detailed proof for one case. The proof of the rest of the cases is similar.

Subcase 1.
T=\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}
, i.e., we have

\begin{array}[]{llll}(i)&\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}::K^{\kappa}&\quad\mathsf{where}\,\,K^{\kappa}=u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\\
&\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{2}::K^{\kappa}&\\[4.0pt]
(ii)&\Delta^{\lambda}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}::K^{\lambda}&\quad\mathsf{where}\,\,\Delta^{\lambda}=\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\\
&\Delta^{\lambda}\Vdash\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{2}::K^{\lambda}&\\
\end{array}

By the assumptions of this case, we know that there is a message sent along
u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}{:}A\otimes B
in
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}
that is received by a process in
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})
and

\begin{array}[]{l}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}},\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\;\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}.\par\end{array}

By
\dagger_{\kappa}
,
Lem.

29
, and
\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})\mapsto^{0_{\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\kappa};\Theta^{\prime}_{\kappa}}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})
, we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\,\exists\Upsilon_{\kappa_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{\kappa_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\,\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\kappa}\subseteq\Upsilon_{\kappa_{2}}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\kappa}.\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1});\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta^{\prime}_{\kappa}.\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbf{then}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1});\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}\end{array}

In particular, we know that
u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle)
and
u_{\alpha}\in\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\kappa}
, which gives us:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1});\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}\Vdash u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\rrbracket_{\cdot;u_{\alpha}}^{m+1}\end{array}

By
Row
r_{2}
.
of the logical relation, we have for
k_{2}\in L
such that
k_{1}=k_{2}
if
c\sqsubseteq\xi

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{2}=\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2}),\,\mathsf{and}\\
\circ^{\prime}\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\kappa}_{1}\Vdash u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\rrbracket^{m},\\
\end{array}

where
B=A_{k_{1}}=A_{k_{2}}
, which by well-typedness we know it’s true.

Next, we consider
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}
.
By
\dagger_{\lambda}
,
Lem.

29
, and
\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}\mapsto^{0_{\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{ll}\,\exists\Upsilon_{\lambda_{2}}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{2}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{\lambda_{2}}}}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon^{\prime}_{\lambda}.\\
\quad\,\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda}\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{m+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda}).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta^{\prime}_{\lambda}.\\
\,\quad\mathbf{then}\,\,\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\lambda},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda}\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{m+1}\end{array}

In particular, we know that
u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda})
and also
u_{\alpha}\in\Theta^{\prime}_{\lambda}
, which gives us:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1};\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket\Delta^{\lambda^{\prime}},u_{\alpha}{:}\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash K^{\lambda}\rrbracket_{u_{\alpha};\cdot}^{m+1}\end{array}

By
Row
l_{2}
.
of the logical relation, knowing that
k_{1}=k_{2}
if
c\sqsubseteq\xi
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\forall m.\,(\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1});\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta^{\lambda},u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\Vdash K^{\lambda}\rrbracket^{m}.\\
\end{array}

By forward closure (
Lem.

27
) and
\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{1})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{1}
we have:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\circ^{\prime\prime}\,\,\forall m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{1};\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta,u_{\alpha+1}{:}B\Vdash K\rrbracket^{m}\\
\end{array}

By induction on the number of steps,
\circ^{\prime}
,
\circ^{\prime\prime}
, and
\dagger_{n}
for
n\neq\kappa,\lambda
we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\Upsilon_{1},\Theta_{1},\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}.\,\mathbf{if}\,{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}}\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1};\Theta_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}\\
\,\mathbf{then}\,\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\[2.0pt]
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\[2.0pt]
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

By a
\forall\mathbf{E}.
and
{\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime}}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{1}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{1}\}_{n\in I_{2}}}\mapsto^{{j^{\prime}}_{\Upsilon_{1}}}{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}
we get:

\begin{array}[]{l}\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(u_{\alpha}^{\langle c,e\rangle}.k_{2})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

We also know that

\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I}=\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\lambda}_{2}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\mapsto^{*}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\beta}\,u_{\alpha})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}.

We get the following for the same
\Upsilon_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
:

\begin{array}[]{l}\exists\Upsilon_{2},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{such\,that}\,\,\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{1}}\mathcal{B}^{\kappa^{\prime\prime}}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}y_{\beta}\,u_{\alpha})\mathcal{B}^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{2}\{\mathcal{B}^{n}_{2}\}_{n\in I_{2}}\mapsto^{{*}_{\Upsilon_{2}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\Upsilon_{1}\subseteq\Upsilon_{2}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{Out}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Upsilon_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{\cdot;x_{\gamma}}^{k^{\prime}+1}\,\mathsf{and}\\
\;\;\forall\,x_{\gamma}\in\mathbf{In}(\Delta\Vdash K).\,\mathbf{if}\,x_{\gamma}\in\Theta_{1}.\,\mathbf{then}\,\,(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1};\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}_{\Psi_{0}}^{\xi}\llbracket\Delta\Vdash K\rrbracket_{x_{\gamma};\cdot}^{k^{\prime}+1}.\end{array}

and the proof of this subcase is complete.

∎

15
Adequacy as a bisimulation

Internal transition
\xrightarrow{\tau}
defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime}\,\mathsf{iff}\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}

Actions
\xrightarrow{\overline{y_{\alpha}}\,q}
,
\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{L}\,y_{\alpha}\,q}}
and
\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{R}\,y_{\alpha}\,q}}
defined as below when
y\in\mathbf{fn}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
:

\begin{array}[]{llll}(1)\,\mathcal{D}_{1}{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,y_{\alpha})}\,{\mathcal{D}_{2}}&\xrightarrow{\overline{y_{\alpha}}\,\mathbf{close}}&\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}\\[10.0pt]
(2)\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\overline{y_{\alpha}}\,k}&\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}\\[10.0pt]
(3)\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}\,y_{\alpha})\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\overline{y_{\alpha}}\,x_{\beta}}&\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{2}\\[10.0pt]
(4)\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},\mathbf{wait}\,y_{\alpha};P){\mathcal{D}_{2}}&\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{L}\,y_{\alpha}\,\mathbf{close}}}&{\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,y_{\alpha})\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},\mathbf{wait}\,y_{\alpha};P)\,\mathcal{D}_{2}}\\[10.0pt]
(5)\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},\mathbf{case}\,y_{\alpha}\,(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in I})\,\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{L}\,y_{\alpha}\,k}}&{\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}.k)\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},\mathbf{case}\,y_{\alpha}\,(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in I})\mathcal{D}_{2}}\\[10.0pt]
(6)\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,y_{\alpha})\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{L}\,y_{\alpha}\,x_{\beta}}}&\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}\,y_{\alpha})\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{proc}(z_{\delta},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,y_{\alpha})\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\\[10.0pt]
(7)\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y_{\alpha}\,(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in I})\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{R}\,y_{\alpha}\,k}}&\mathcal{D}_{1}\,\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha},\mathbf{case}\,y_{\alpha}\,(\ell\Rightarrow P_{\ell})_{\ell\in I})\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(y_{\alpha}.k)\\[10.0pt]
(8)\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,y_{\alpha})\,\mathcal{D}_{2}&\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{R}\,y_{\alpha}\,x_{\beta}}}&\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathbf{proc}(y_{\alpha},w\leftarrow\mathbf{recv}\,y_{\alpha})\mathcal{D}_{2}\,\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,x_{\beta}\,y_{\alpha})\end{array}

Figure 21:
The transition rules. An overline indicates that an outgoing message, an otherwise the message is incoming.

Definition 32

(Free names of a configuration)
.

for
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{D}::\Delta^{\prime}
, we define
\mathsf{fn}(\mathcal{D})
as
\mathit{dom}(\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})
.

Definition 33

(Weak transition relations)
.

(1)

\xRightarrow{}
is the reflexive and transitive closure of
\xrightarrow{\tau}
.

(2)

\xRightarrow{\alpha}
is
\xRightarrow{}\xrightarrow{\alpha}
.

Definition 34

(Asynchronous bisimilarity)
.

Asynchronous bisimilarity is the largest symmetric relation such that whenever
\mathcal{D}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{D}_{2}
, we have

1.

(\tau-\mathtt{step})
if
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
then
\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\xRightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2},

2.

(\mathsf{output})
if
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{\overline{x_{\alpha}}\,q}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
then
\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\xRightarrow{\overline{x_{\alpha}}\,q}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.

3.

(\mathsf{left\,input})
for all
q\not\in\mathsf{fn}(\mathcal{D}_{1}).
, if
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{L}\,x_{\alpha}\,q}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
then
\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\xRightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathbf{msg}(x_{\alpha}.q)\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2},

4.

(\mathsf{right\,input})
for all
q\not\in\mathsf{fn}(\mathcal{D}_{1}).
, if
\mathcal{D}_{1}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{R}\,x_{\alpha}\,q}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}
then
\exists\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}.\,\mathcal{D}_{2}\xRightarrow{\tau}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathbf{msg}(x_{\alpha}.q).

where
\mathbf{msg}(x_{\alpha}.q)
is defined as
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{close}\,x_{\alpha})
if
q=\mathbf{close}
,
\mathbf{msg}(x_{\alpha}.k)
if
q=k
, and
\mathbf{msg}(\mathbf{send}\,z_{\delta}\,x_{\alpha})
if
q=z_{\delta}
.

Definition 35

(Typing context projections -integrity)
.

Downward projection on linear contexts and
K
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lclc}\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&\mathsf{if}\;e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\cdot\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\cdot&\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\sqsubseteq\xi\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi&:=&\_{:}1\langle\top,\top\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\end{array}

Definition 36

(Typing context projections -confidentiality)
.

Downward projection on linear contexts and
K
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lclc}\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;c\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&:=&\Delta\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&\mathsf{if}\;c\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\cdot\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&:=&\cdot&\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&:=&x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;c\sqsubseteq\xi\\
x_{\alpha}{:}T\langle c,e\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi&:=&\_{:}1\langle\top,\top\rangle&\mathsf{if}\;c\not\sqsubseteq\xi\\
\end{array}

Definition 37

(High confidentiality provider and High confidentiality client)
.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\cdot\,\,\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\cdot)\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&c\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\mathcal{T}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\\
&&\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\mathcal{T}\in\mathsf{Tree}(\cdot\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle),\mathbf{or}\\
&&c\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\\[10.0pt]
\mathcal{T}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&c\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{T}\in\mathsf{Tree}(x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash\_:1\langle\top,\top\rangle),\mathbf{or}\\
&&c\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{T}=\cdot\end{array}

Definition 38

(Related nigh integrity provider and client)
.

\begin{array}[]{lll}(\cdot,\cdot)\,\,\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\cdot)\\[5.0pt]
(\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta,x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{T}_{i}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta),\mathbf{or}\\[3.0pt]
&&e\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{T}_{i}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\\[0.0pt]
&&\,\,\quad\quad\mathsf{and}\,\,\cdot\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}\equiv_{\xi}^{\Psi_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{2}::x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle.\\[10.0pt]
(\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(\_\langle\top,\top\rangle)\\[5.0pt]
(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle)&\mathsf{iff}&e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,,\mathbf{or}\\
&&e\sqsubseteq\xi\,\mathsf{and}\,x_{\alpha}{:}A\langle c,e\rangle\Vdash\mathcal{T}_{1}\equiv_{\xi}^{\Psi_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{2}::\_:1\langle\top,\top\rangle\end{array}

Definition 39
.

For
\mathcal{D}_{1}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{1}}\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
,
\mathcal{D}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{2}}\Vdash y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)
we define

\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\approx^{\xi}_{a}\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\,\,\mathsf{as}

\begin{array}[]{ll}\Delta=\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\,K=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\,K^{\prime}=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\forall\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}),\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}),\\
\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CCLient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle),\,\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle).\\
\mathsf{if}(\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta^{\prime}\backslash\Delta)\,\mathsf{and}\,(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(K^{\prime}\backslash K)\\
\mathsf{then}\,|\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}|\approx_{a}|\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}|.\end{array}

Theorem 40
.

For all
\mathcal{D}_{1}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{1}}\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1})
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{2}}\Vdash y_{\beta}{:}A_{2})
, if

(\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}[{c_{1}}])\equiv^{\Psi_{0}}_{\xi}(\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}[{c_{2}}])
then
(\Delta_{1}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{1}::x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}[{c_{1}}])\approx_{a}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}\Vdash\mathcal{D}_{2}::y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}[{c_{2}}])
.

Proof.

Our goal is to show that

(\star_{1})\,\,\,\Delta=\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=\Delta_{2},\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\,K=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}

(\star^{\prime}_{1})\,\,\,\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\,K^{\prime}=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi\,\,\,\,\,\mathsf{and}

(\star_{2})\,\,\,\forall\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}),\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}),

\quad\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle),\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle).

\quad\,\mathsf{if}(\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta)\,\mathsf{and}\,(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(K)\,

\quad\,\mathsf{then}\,\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}.

We know that

\begin{array}[]{lll}{\mathcal{D}_{1}}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{1}}\Vdash x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,{\mathcal{D}_{2}}\in\mathsf{Tree}({\Delta_{2}}\Vdash y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta_{2}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=\Delta^{\prime}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}\xi=K^{\prime}\,\,\mathsf{and}\\
\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}).\,\forall\,\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}).\\
\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle).\,\forall\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle).\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta^{\prime}}\Vdash{K^{\prime}}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\\[1.0pt]
\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta^{\prime}}\Vdash{K^{\prime}}\rrbracket^{m}.\end{array}

The first part of the goal (
\star_{1}
) is starightforward from the assumption, the defintions of
\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}
and
\Downarrow^{\mathsf{ig}}
and the fact that the min integrity (
e
) of a channel is always bounded by its max secrecy (
c
) in well-typed processes, i.e.,
e\sqsubseteq c
and thus
c\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow e\sqsubseteq\xi
. We get
\Delta=\Delta_{1}\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=\Delta_{2},\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi\,\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,\,K=y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi=x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\Downarrow^{\mathsf{cf}}\xi
, such that
\Delta\subseteq\Delta^{\prime}
and
K\subseteq K^{\prime}
. The part (
\star^{\prime}_{1}
) is given by the assumption.

To prove the second part of the goal (
\star_{2}
) we apply a forall introduction to get arbitrary configurations

\mathcal{B}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1})
,
\mathcal{B}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2})
,
\mathcal{T}_{1}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle)
,
\mathcal{T}_{2}\in\mathbf{H\text{-}CClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)
. We assume that
(\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta^{\prime}\backslash\Delta)
and
(\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2})\in\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(K^{\prime}\backslash K)
. We observe that by the tree invariant for any channel in
\Delta_{i}
, the min integrity (
e
) is always bounded by the max secrecy (
c
), i.e.,
e\sqsubseteq c
, and thus
c\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow e\sqsubseteq\xi
and
e\not\sqsubseteq\xi\rightarrow c\not\sqsubseteq\xi
. Similarly,
e_{1}\sqsubseteq c_{1}
and
e_{2}\sqsubseteq c_{2}
. Thus we can rewrite
\mathcal{B}_{i}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}
as
\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
and
\mathcal{T}_{i}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{i}
, such that
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{1}),\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta_{2}),\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}ICLient}^{\xi}(x_{\alpha}{:}A_{1}\langle c_{1},e_{1}\rangle),\,\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbf{L\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(y_{\beta}{:}A_{2}\langle c_{2},e_{2}\rangle)
.

Moreover, by the definition of
\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IProvider}^{\xi}(\Delta^{\prime}\backslash\Delta)
and
\mathbf{Hrel\text{-}IClient}^{\xi}(K^{\prime}\backslash K)
, we get that all trees in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
are related by the logical relation, and also
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
are related by the logical relation (for every
m
).

With
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{i}
, we instantiate the for all quantifier in the assumption and we get

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta^{\prime}}\Vdash{K^{\prime}}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\\[1.0pt]
\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta^{\prime}}\Vdash{K^{\prime}}\rrbracket^{m}.\end{array}

Now, we can use relatedness of the trees in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and relatedness of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
and the compositionality lemma
31
to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{\mathcal{}}{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{T}^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\\[1.0pt]
\forall\,m.\,(\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}.\end{array}

Observe that all channels in
\Delta
and
K
have lower max secrecy
c
than the observer
\xi
. In this special case, the term interpretation of our integrity logical relation implies the term interpretation of the logical relation for equivalence of programs that has been introduced in prior work
[
7
]
, and proved to be sound with respect to bisimulation.
We apply
Lem.

41
, on our goal to get

\begin{array}[]{l}\forall\,m.\,(|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}|,|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{\mathcal{}}{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}{T}^{\prime}_{2}|)\in\mathcal{E}^{c}\llbracket{|\Delta|}\Vdash{|K|}\rrbracket^{m},\,\mathsf{and}\\[1.0pt]
\forall\,m.\,(|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{2}|,|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{1}|)\in\mathcal{E}^{c}\llbracket{|\Delta|}\Vdash{|K|}\rrbracket^{m}.\end{array}

Now, we can apply
Lem.

42
to get
|\mathcal{B}_{1}\mathcal{D}_{1}\mathcal{T}_{1}|\approx_{a}|\mathcal{B}_{2}\mathcal{D}_{2}\mathcal{T}_{2}|
and the proof is complete.
∎

\begin{array}[]{lcl}(1)\;({\mathcal{D}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}_{2}})&\mathsf{iff}&({\mathcal{D}_{1}};{\mathcal{D}_{2}})\in\mathsf{Tree}(\cdot\Vdash y_{\alpha})\,\mathsf{and}\\

Figure 22:
Term and value interpretation of the recursive session logical relation for equivalence of programs with confidentiality interface from prior work
[
7
]
.

To be self-contained, we have included the term and value interpretations of the logical relation introduced in prior work
[
7
]
in
Fig.

22
. This logical relation from previous work is defined considering the vanilla session typed system, meaning that there are no security annotations in the configurations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
,
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and the interface
\Delta
and
K
. The authors use an erasure function to erase the confidentiality annotations from the configurations and the context. We assume a similar erasure function
|\_|
, which removes all the confidentiality and integrity annotations from the configuration and the context. For the sake of simplicity, in the next two lemmas, we use the same notations for configurations (e.g.
\mathcal{D}
) and the context (e.g.
\Delta
) before and after the erasure.
The authors use the tree predicate, to state that both configurations are well-typed in the vanilla session type system.

The next theorem demonstrates that the term interpretation of our integrity logical relation implies the term interpretation of the logical relation introduced in prior work
[
7
]
.

Lemma 41
.

If
\forall\,m.(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m}
and every channel in
\Delta
and
K
are observable, i.e., if
x_{\alpha}{:}A_{\langle}c_{1},e_{1}\rangle\in\Delta,K
then
c_{1}\sqsubseteq\xi
, then
\forall\,m.(|\mathcal{D}_{1}|,|\mathcal{D}_{2}|)\in\mathcal{E}^{c}\llbracket{|\Delta|}\Vdash{|K|}\rrbracket^{m}
, where
|\_|
is the erasure function that removes all the confidentiality and integrity annotations from the configuration and the context.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the index
m
. For
m=0
, the proof is straightforward as all processes in our system are well-typed in the vanilla type system.
For
m=m^{\prime}+1
, we observe that it’s enough to show (a) if
(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}+1}_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}
then
(|\mathcal{D}_{1}|,|\mathcal{D}_{2}|)\in\mathcal{V}^{c}\llbracket{|\Delta|}\Vdash{|K|}\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}+1}_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}
and (b) if
(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\xi}_{\Psi_{0}}\llbracket{\Delta}\Vdash{K}\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}+1}_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}
then
(|\mathcal{D}_{1}|,|\mathcal{D}_{2}|)\in\mathcal{V}^{c}\llbracket{|\Delta|}\Vdash{|K|}\rrbracket^{m^{\prime}+1}_{\cdot;y_{\alpha}}
. The proof of the two statements proceed by case analysis on the type of
y_{\alpha}
. Again, the proof is straightforward by observing that the definition of all cases in our integrity logical relation implies the ones in
Fig.

22
. In particular, for the cases responsible for communicating labels,
(r_{2})
,
(r_{3})
,
(l_{2})
, and
(l_{3})
in
Figs.

18
and
19
we know that the same lables
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
are sent/received since all the channels in
\Delta
and
K
are observable. This means that they imply lines
(2)
,
(3)
,
(7)
, and
(8)
in
Fig.

22
resp.
∎

Finally, we state the soundness result from the TR of
[
7
]
that shows the term interpretation of the confidentiality logical relation is sound with respect to the asynchronous bisimulation.

Lemma 42

(Lemma 24 from the TR of
[
7
]
)
.

Consider a pair of vanilla session-typed forests
\mathcal{C}_{1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}
with
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{1}::\Delta^{\prime}
and
\Delta\Vdash\mathcal{C}_{2}::\Delta^{\prime}
(in the vanilla session typed system) consisting of multiple session-typed trees indexed in the set
I
, i.e.,
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{\mathcal{C}^{j}_{i}\}_{j\in I}
and
\Delta^{j}\Vdash\mathcal{C}^{j}_{i}::K^{j}
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
, with
\Delta=\{\Delta^{j}\}_{j\in I}
and
\Delta^{\prime}=\{K^{j}\}_{j\in I}
. We have:

\forall j\in I.\forall m.\,(\mathcal{C}^{j}_{1},\mathcal{C}^{j}_{2})\in\mathcal{E}\llbracket\Delta^{j}\Vdash K^{j}\rrbracket^{m}\,\,\mathsf{and}\,\,(\mathcal{C}^{j}_{2},\mathcal{C}^{j}_{1})\in\mathcal{E}\llbracket\Delta^{j}\Vdash K^{j}\rrbracket^{m}\,\,\mathsf{iff}\,\,\mathcal{C}_{1}\approx_{a}\mathcal{C}_{2}.

Proof.

For the proof please see Lem. 24 from the technical report of
[
7
]
.
∎

16
Implementation

16.1
Overview

We implemented a
type checker
for
\mathsf{SINTEGRITY}
, whose features we now briefly describe.
Our base calculus is an intuitionistic session-typed process calculus, for which type checking is well
understood and implementations exist
[
25
,
22
]
. The novelty of our work lies
in our algorithmic treatment of the IFC fragment of the calculus, which as far as we know is new
and has not been attempted yet.

16.1.1
User-specified security semilattice.

Our type checker allows the user to specify a
concrete security join-semilattice
\Psi_{0}
(see
Def.

4
) and verify its validity. It will reject security levels that
fail the semilattice axioms. To specify
\Psi_{0}
, the user must prefix the source code with a

secrecy
section, where the user must list the elements of the lattice in decreasing order of secrecy.
In other words, elements must be listed by their topological order,
if we were to view the lattice as a directed graph where arrows point from high secrecy to low secrecy. For each
element, the programmer must then list its immediate predecessors.
For example, we indicate our banking example lattice as follows, where
#bank
stands for the top element:

⬇

#bank

<

()

#alice

<

(
#bank
)

#bob

<

(
#bank
)

#guest

<

(
#alice
,

#bob
)

end

Lemma 43
.

Suppose the user lists the elements of an
n
-element lattice with a sequence
(e_{0},\cdots,e_{n-1})
.
Define
L_{i}=(e_{0},\cdots,e_{i})
for
0\leq i<n
(
L_{i}
is the preorder obtained by taking the
first
i
elements specified along with arrows between them). Then the full preorder
L_{n-1}
is a
join-semilattice iff for all
i<n
,
L_{i}
is a join-semilattice.

Proof.

The
if
part is trivial (take
i=n-1
).
It remains to be shown that if
L_{n-1}
is a semilattice then for all
i<n
,
L_{i}
is a semilattice.
We note that we will show if
L_{i}
is a semilattice then
L_{i-1}
is a semilattice.
We sketch the proof, following a similar argument as in
[
34
]
:
L_{i-1}
is obtained
by removing one of the minimal elements from
L_{i}
. Therefore, all semilattice axioms are preserved for
the remaining elements, concluding
L_{i-1}
is by definition a semilattice. From here by an argument
of induction (base case is just
L_{n-1}
, we show that for all
i<n
,
L_{i}
is a join-semilattice).
∎

The lemma proves that any semilattice can be specified by listing its elements in topological order,
and verifying that each increment extends the prior semilattice to form another semilattice. This can be done algorithmically
within reasonable computational complexity: at every step we keep track of the minimal elements
in the current semilattice. For every additional element we ensure that it has a join with every
existing minimal element. This can be done efficiently by computing the

least common ancestor
and check uniqueness (a problem with known fast solutions
[
42
]
).
The type checker implemented a more expensive but straightforward method of directly checking
the definition of supremum, because the number of elements in a lattice is generally small.

16.1.2
Lattice theories for polymorphic process definitions

Our type checker allows the programmer
to specify any number of
lattice theories
, as defined in
Def.

4
. A lattice theory postulates a series of security terms
c,d\in\mathcal{S}
,
ranging over the elements of the concrete security lattice as well as a set of security variables
\psi\in\mathcal{V}
, along with a list of
relations
E\in\mathcal{E}
that the variables must satisfy.
As defined in
Def.

4
,
E
may only be of the form
c\sqsubseteq d
,
but the implementation also allows programmers to specify
equality between formulas (e.g.,
c\sqcup d=e\sqcup\texttt{Alice}
),
which is desugared into two
\sqsubseteq
relations
(
c\sqcup d\sqsubseteq e\sqcup\texttt{Alice}
, and vice versa). Lattice theories are named.

A process is then parametrized over a lattice theory of the programmer’s choice.
By postulating
a lattice theory a process gains access to the security variables within, and therefore can specify
security terms involving these variables.

During the type checking we will encounter relations that we must establish. The type checker
implements a search algorithm that automatically derives the desired relation from
the induction definition in
Fig.

16
,
and if it fails to do the process is rejected.
The relation it can attempt to show includes
\sqsubseteq
and equality of security terms (again
treated as conjunction of separate
\sqsubseteq
relations). The latter is useful because this allows
the programmer to write down intuitively “equivalent but not literally equal” secrecy terms (e.g.,
\textsf{Alice}\sqcup\textsf{Bob}
versus
Bank
) and have it type checked.
The same mechanism is also used to validate security theory substitution during spawning.

The type checker will insist that the user pick a main process and provide a substitution of its security
theory from the concrete lattice (e.g.,
execute P[#bank, #alice, #bob, #guest]
), thus establishing validity of the security theories of all subsequently
referenced process definitions. This is crucial as the user can postulate secrecy theories that are
inconsistent with the concrete lattice. Processes that assume these theories
will
leak information
if executed. However, our type system prevents their execution because no substitution from the concrete
lattice exists. This is akin to the fact that types such as
\forall t.t
is uninhabited in polymorphic

\lambda
-calculus.

16.1.3
Mutual recursive definition

The type checker also implements limited support for
equi-recurisve definitions, in line with the rules laid out in the main body of the paper.
In particular, for (recursive) type definitions, although definitions can refer to each other, they must
all be individually
contractive
. Furthermore, it should be emphasized that each definition is
a recursive data type definition, not to be confused with a
type definition

(i.e.
type t = ...
declaration in Standard ML).
Because all definitions are implicitly mutually recursive, the type checker will reject attempts to shadow
previous definitions.

16.1.4
Synchronization pattern check

The type checker implements the synchronization check described
in
§​

10.2
. The check will first attempt to apply the unsynchronized rules.
If any of the rules apply, the type checker commits to that choice.
If none of the rules apply, the type checker attempts to apply the synchronized rules,
rejecting the program when this fails.
This algorithm is fine because the unsynchronized rules are
invertible
.

The synchronization type checker also employ a few optimizations that are justified by transitivity and
symmetry of the
\Psi\vDash P\sim_{\langle d,f\rangle}Q
relation. Example 1: in type checking
rule
\oplus_{L}
, instead of checking all pairs of branches, we will arbitrarily pick one branch and
check this against all other branches, relying on transitivity and symmetry to conclude the rest.
Example 2: in checking
RcvLab
, we will pick one branch from
P_{j}
and
Q_{k}
each and
compare them. This is safe because the type checker will establish the relation within branches of

P_{j}
and
Q_{k}
respectively, and by an argument of transitivity and symmetry we conclude the rest.

16.1.5
Implementation

The type checker is written in OCaml.
It offers a list of existing examples and produces type checking outputs. The
application itself is hosted statically by the GitHub Pages service.

16.1.6
Guide to the concrete syntax

Here we describe the concrete syntax used in the implementation.
The syntax differs from the one in the main part of the paper,
which is more compact for the purpose of formal development.

16.1.7
General structure

A valid input file of
\mathsf{SINTEGRITY}
must contain the following sections,
in this order,

•

A specification of the concrete lattice (
secrecy

...

end
);

•

One or more lattice theories (
theory
[...]

...

end
);

•

A session type definition signature (
stype

signature

...

end
);

•

A process definition signature (
proc

signature

...

end
);

•

And the main process to be executed (
exec

P
[...]
)

The language uses ML-style comments
(* comment *)
, and it is entirely
whitespace (indentation) insensitive.

The language has no restriction in terms of identifier capitalization. However, it is
conventional to capitalize the session type names, process names and labels.

We will now introduce the “official” syntax of the language. The author acknowledges that
people often have their own preferences, therefore the language supports a few “unofficial”
variations for a subset of the keywords.

16.1.8
Concrete lattice

Concrete lattices are specified by entries
within
secrecy

...

end
. Each entry defines a concrete secrecy level
and immediate provide its nearest ancestors, listed in the shape of a tuple.
Concrete secrecy levels must start with a pound (
#
). The levels
must be listed in topological order (i.e. they may only refer to levels that
are already present).

The following defines the concrete lattice used for our running example.
Here
#bank
is known to be the top element because it has no ancestor.

⬇

#bank

<

()

#alice

<

(
#bank
)

#bob

<

(
#bank
)

#guest

<

(
#alice
,

#bob
)

end

16.1.9
Secrecy theories

The programmer may postulate a number of lattice
theories. For each theory the programmer must provide a name and list the
security variables. Then the programmer may list one or more relations, separated by
a semi colon. Each equation relates two
security terms
.

The following chart lists the syntax for expressing security terms.

{{{\begin{array}[]{llc@{\quad\extracolsep{\fill}}lll}\text{Security terms}&c,d&\mathrel{::=}&\textbf{alice}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_directive ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{\#alice}}}}}}}}}&\text{concrete levels}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&a&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{a}}}}}}}}}&\text{variables}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&c\sqcup d&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{c}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbar{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{\textunderscore}}}\textbar{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{d}}}}}}}}}&\text{join of terms}\\
\end{array}

For example, the following theory postulate a theory
Th
with variables
a
and
b
.
From the relations postulated the type checker is capable of deducing
a\sqsubseteq b
.

⬇

a

<=

#alice
;

#alice

|
_
|

#bob

<=

b
;

end

16.1.10
Type signature

Session type signature block contains definitions of
(possibly mutual recursive) session types. The block itself contains zero or more session type
definitions.

The following block defines two mutually recursive session types
A
and
B
.

⬇

stype

A

=

1

*

B

stype

B

=

1

*

A

end

The following chart lists concrete syntax for session types.

{{{{{{\begin{array}[]{llc@{\quad\extracolsep{\fill}}lll}\text{Session types}&A,B&\mathrel{::=}&A&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{A}}}}}}}}}&\text{name of session type}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\mathbf{1}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{unit}}}}}}}}}&\text{closing session}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\oplus\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}+{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbackslash\textbraceleft{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{l}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}-\textgreater{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{A\textunderscore l}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbar{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}...\textbackslash\textbraceright}}}}}}&\text{internal choice}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\&\{\ell{:}A_{\ell}\}_{\ell\in L}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\&{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbackslash\textbraceleft{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{l}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}-\textgreater{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{A\textunderscore l}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbar{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}...\textbackslash\textbraceright}}}}}}&\text{external choice}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&A\otimes B&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{A}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textasteriskcentered{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{B}}}}}}}}}&\text{channel output}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&A\multimap B&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{A}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}-{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{o}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{B}}}}}}}}}&\text{channel input}\\
\end{array}

16.1.11
Process signature

Process signature block contains definition of
(possibly mutual recursive) process definitions. The block contains zero or more process
definitions. To define process
X
:

\Psi;y_{1}{:}B_{1}\langle\psi_{1},\omega_{1}\rangle,\dots,y_{n}{:}B_{n}\langle\psi_{n},\omega_{n}\rangle\vdash_{\Sigma}X=P@\langle\psi_{0},\omega_{0}\rangle::x{:}A\langle\psi,\omega\rangle

The programmer writes down the following signature

⬇

using

(
y1

:

B1
[
psi1
,

w1
],

...)

at

[
psi0
,

w0
]

=

...

where
X
is the process name;
Psi
is the name of the lattice theory it assumes;
x

is the name of its offering channel and
A
[
psi
,

w
]
is confidentiality and integrity of
x
;

[
psi0
,

w0
]
is the level that the process is spawned at, and
y1

:

B1
[
psi1
,

w1
],

...
are
its arguments, and the dots stands for the code
P
of the process. The
\Sigma
context is
the entire process definition context.

If a channel does not utilize regrading (that is its integrity coincides with is confidentiality),
the programmer may specify
A
[
psi
]
meaning
A
[
psi
,

psi
]
. This is just a quality-of-life
feature.

The following table lists syntax for process terms (spawning described in text separately):

{{{{{{{\begin{array}[]{llc@{\quad\extracolsep{\fill}}lll}\text{Proc term}&P,Q&\mathrel{::=}&x.k;P&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{select}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{K}}};{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{P}}}}}}}}}&\text{send label}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\mathbf{case}\,x(\ell{\Rightarrow}P_{\ell})_{\ell\in L}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{case}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbackslash\textbraceleft{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{l}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}-\textgreater{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{P\textunderscore x}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}\textbar{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}...\textbackslash\textbraceright}}}}}}&\text{recieve label}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\mathbf{send}\,y\,x;P&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{send}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{to}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}};{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{P}}}}}}}}}&\text{send channel}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&z{\leftarrow}\mathbf{recv}\,x;P_{z}&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}={\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{receive}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{z}}};{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{P}}}}}}}}}&\text{receive channel}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\mathbf{wait}\,x;P&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{wait}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}};{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{P}}}}}}}}}&\text{wait on channel}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&\mathbf{close}\,x&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{close}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}}}}}}}&\text{close channel}\\
&&\mathrel{\phantom{\mid}}&x\leftarrow y&{\sloppy\text{\hbox{\pagecolor{background_color}\lstinline{{\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@numbers\lst@@@set@frame\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@rulecolor\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\lst@@@set@language\small{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{forward}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{y}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_keyword}{\color[rgb]{0.25,0.3906,1}{to}}}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_space}{ }}{\@listingGroup{ltx_lst_identifier}{{x}}}}}}}}}&\text{forward channel}\\
\end{array}

Within the process body it has access to the security variables postulated in its signature.

To spawn
(x^{\langle c,e\rangle}\leftarrow X[\gamma]\leftarrow\Delta)@\langle c_{0},e_{0}\rangle;Q_{x}
, the programmer writes

Here the programmer instantiates the process of definition
X
,
supplying the lattice theory substitution
\gamma
given by
c
,

...
,
one security term for every variable in the theory assumed by

X
, in the order they are listed in the theory. The security term can use variables defined in the
spawner’s theory. The arguments
\Delta
are supplied in the parentheses as
x
,

...
. The type checker automatically
infers the confidentiality and initial running confidentiality of the spawnee.

The following signature defines two processes that spawn each other. This assumes the availability
of the security variable
a
in the theory
Th
. Both processes do not require arguments,
and do not use regrading.

⬇

proc

M1
[
Th
]

provide

(
x

:

unit
[
a
])

using

()

at

[
a
]

=

instantiate

y

=

M2
[]();

forward

y

to

x

proc

M2
[
Th
]

provide

(
x

:

unit
[
a
])

using

()

at

[
a
]

=

instantiate

y

=

M1
[]();

forward

y

to

x

end

16.1.12
Main process

The programmer must designate a process
P
as the main process
and provide a substitution from the concrete lattice into the lattice theory of
P

(the security terms
c
,
d
can only refer to the concrete lattice). Doing
so completes the input file.

17
extra examples

17.1
Polymorphic banking example

\Psi;y_{1}{:}\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi,\mathbf{guest}\rangle},y_{2}{:}\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi^{\prime},\mathbf{guest}\rangle},w_{1}{:}\mathsf{account}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi,\psi\rangle},w_{2}{:}\mathsf{account}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\rangle}\vdash\mathsf{Bank}::x{:}\mathsf{1}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\mathbf{bank},\mathbf{bank}\rangle}

x\leftarrow\mathsf{Bank}\leftarrow y_{1},y_{2},w_{1},w_{2}=

\mathbf{case}\,y_{1}\,

(\mathit{tok_{white}}\Rightarrow y_{1}.\mathit{succ};\,

(u\leftarrow\mathsf{Statement}[\gamma]\leftarrow\cdot);\,\mathbf{send}\,u\,w_{1};(x^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{Bank}[\gamma^{\prime}]\leftarrow y_{2},y_{1},w_{2},w_{1});\;x\leftarrow x^{\prime}

\mid\mathit{tok_{black}}\Rightarrow y_{1}.\mathit{fail};(x^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{Bank}[\gamma^{\prime}]\leftarrow y_{2},y_{1},w_{2},w_{1});\;x\leftarrow x^{\prime}\,){\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\langle\mathbf{guest},\mathbf{guest}\rangle}

\Psi;\cdot\vdash\mathsf{Customer}::y{:}\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi,\mathbf{guest}\rangle}

y\leftarrow\mathsf{Customer}\leftarrow\cdot=

y.\mathit{tok_{white}};\mathbf{case}\,y\,

(\mathit{succ}\Rightarrow(y^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{Customer}[\gamma]\leftarrow\cdot);\;y\leftarrow y^{\prime}

\mid\mathit{fail}\Rightarrow(y^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{Customer}[\gamma]\leftarrow\cdot);\;y\leftarrow y^{\prime})\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\langle l,\mathbf{guest}\rangle}

\Psi;\cdot\vdash\mathsf{Statement}::u{:}\mathsf{transfer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\psi,\psi\rangle}

u\leftarrow\mathsf{Statement}\leftarrow\cdot=\;\;

u.\mathit{transaction};\mathbf{close}\,u\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\langle\psi,\psi\rangle}

\Psi_{0};w_{1}{:}\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\mathbf{alice},\mathbf{alice}\rangle},w_{2}{:}\mathsf{customer}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\mathbf{bob},\mathbf{bob}\rangle}\vdash\mathsf{Main}::x{:}1{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}\langle\mathbf{bank},\mathbf{bank}\rangle}

x\leftarrow\mathsf{Main}\leftarrow w_{1},w_{2}=

(y_{1}\leftarrow\mathsf{Customer}[\gamma_{1}]\leftarrow\cdot);\;(y_{2}\leftarrow\mathsf{Customer}[\gamma^{\prime}_{1}]\leftarrow\cdot);

\;\;(x^{\prime}\leftarrow\mathsf{Bank}[\gamma_{1}]\leftarrow y_{1},y_{2},w_{1},w_{2});\;x\leftarrow x^{\prime}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}@\langle\mathbf{guest},\mathbf{guest}\rangle}

\begin{array}[]{llll}\gamma:=\{\psi\mapsto\psi,\psi^{\prime}\mapsto\psi^{\prime}\}&\gamma_{1}:=\{\psi\mapsto\mathbf{alice},\psi^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{bob}\}\\
\gamma^{\prime}:=\{\psi\mapsto\psi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime}\mapsto\psi\}&\gamma^{\prime}_{1}:=\{\psi\mapsto\mathbf{bob},\psi^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{alice}\}\end{array}
